GSIS Educational Subsidy Program (GESP) (ID: 84) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: GSIS Educational Subsidy Program (GESP)9 
●​ Provider: Government Service Insurance System (GSIS)9 
●​ Category: Government / Dependent Affiliation / Financial Subsidy9 
●​ Official Website: https://www.gsis.gov.ph9 
●​ Application Portal: GSIS Touch Mobile App / GSIS Branch Offices9. 
●​ Official Guidelines: GSIS Board Resolution No. 49-2021; GESP Policy Guidelines9 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The GSIS Educational Subsidy Program provides an annual cash financial subsidy of 
₱10,000.00 to qualified dependents of active low-income GSIS members9. The program 
assists public sector employees in financing the college education of their children, supporting 
human capital development in civil service families9. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen. 


--- PAGE 38 ---
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines. 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Year9. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No9. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes9. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible9. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled in a 4- or 5-year college degree program in a 
CHED-recognized HEI9. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Active student status meeting university retention standards9. 
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Passing grade status required)9. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Controlled via GSIS member parent salary grade (GSIS active members 
in lowest Salary Grades prioritized; SG 24 and below)9. 
●​ Age Restrictions: Dependent child must be below 25 years old. 
●​ School Restrictions: CHED-recognized State Universities and Colleges or Private HEIs9. 
●​ Priority Courses: Any undergraduate degree program9. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Parent must be an active GSIS member with updated premium 
contributions9. 
●​ Good Moral: Required. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Selection based on computer-generated ranking prioritizing 
members with lowest salary grade and longest length of service9. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Dependents of active GSIS members9. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No9. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes9. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced per annual GSIS advisory9. 
●​ Closing Date: Specified in annual notice9. 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual9. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–20279. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct cash subsidy provided to scholar9. 


--- PAGE 39 ---
●​ Monthly Stipend: None9. 
●​ Allowance: ₱10,000.00 cash subsidy per Academic Year9. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into cash grant9. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into cash grant9. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: None9. 
●​ Research Allowance: None9. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: None9. 
●​ Return Service: None9. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Official GESP Application Form9. 
2.​ PSA Birth Certificate of nominated child9. 
3.​ Certificate of Employment / Service Record of GSIS member showing Salary Grade9. 
4.​ Certificate of Enrollment / Registration Form from CHED-recognized college9. 
5.​ School grade report / transcript showing passing marks9. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Passing grades in all enrolled subjects9. 
●​ Regular Load: Full credit load per semester9. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing marks9. 
●​ Return Service: None9. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Member parent inactive or in default of GSIS premium payments9. 
●​ Dependent age reaching 25 years old9. 
●​ Student failing any academic subject9. 
●​ Member parent salary grade exceeding ceiling9.

OWWA Education for Development Scholarship 
(EDSP) (ID: 85) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Education for Development Scholarship Program (EDSP)18 
●​ Provider: Overseas Workers Welfare Administration (OWWA)18 
●​ Category: Government / Merit-and-Need / OFW Dependent27 
●​ Official Website: https://owwa.gov.ph9 
●​ Application Portal: https://scholarship.owwa.gov.ph27 
●​ Official Guidelines: OWWA EDSP Guidelines18 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Education for Development Scholarship Program provides merit-based financial grants to 
qualified dependents of active OWWA members pursuing 4- or 5-year baccalaureate degree 
programs in Philippine colleges and universities18. The program supports migrant worker 
families by ensuring their dependents can access quality higher education27. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born Filipino citizen27. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines27. 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Category 1 (Incoming 1st Year Freshmen); Category 2 (2nd to 5th Year 
College Students)18. 


--- PAGE 42 ---
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No (Has distinct tracks for incoming freshmen and ongoing 
2nd-5th year college students)18. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (Under EDSP Category 2)28. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible18. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in a 4- or 5-year baccalaureate 
degree program in an accredited Philippine college or university18. 
●​ Academic Requirements: 
○​ Category 1 (Freshmen): SHS Grade 12 General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 
80.00% with zero failing grades; must qualify via national qualifying examination 
(top DOST national exam takers)27. 
○​ Category 2 (Upperclassmen): Cumulative college GWA of at least 85.00% or 
equivalent with zero failing grades28. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 80.00% (Freshmen entry) / 85.00% (Upperclassmen entry)27. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE27. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE for EDSP main track (Restricted 
by active OWWA member contribution status)18. 
●​ Age Restrictions: Must be single and not over 21 years old for Category 1 (Freshmen)27; 
single and not over 30 years old for Category 2 (Upperclassmen)28. 
●​ School Restrictions: Accredited Philippine-based colleges and universities18. 
●​ Priority Courses: Any 4- or 5-year baccalaureate degree program18. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be a child of an active OWWA member, or a sibling of a 
single / childless active OWWA member18. 
●​ Good Moral: Required27. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: OFW parent/sibling membership must be ACTIVE at the time of 
application18. Only one scholarship beneficiary per OFW family is allowed under OWWA 
scholarship programs27. Single marital status mandatory27. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Dependents of active OWWA members entering Grade 12 / 1st Year 
College or currently in 2nd to 5th Year College27. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes (Incoming 1st Year Freshmen)27. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes (Under Category 2)28. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes (Under Category 2)28. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes (Under Category 2)28. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No18. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes28. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: July 16 annually (for main application intake)28 / November 10 (for 


--- PAGE 43 ---
DOST-EDSP track)27. 
●​ Closing Date: July 31 annually28 / November 2827. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual27. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–202728. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct financial grant disbursed to scholar27. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual financial assistance package27. 
●​ Allowance: Financial assistance of ₱60,000.00 per Academic Year (disbursed at 
₱30,000.00 per semester)18. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into financial assistance27. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into financial assistance27. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: None18. 
●​ Research Allowance: None18. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: None18. 
●​ Return Service: None18. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Proof of OFW Active Membership (OWWA Membership verification printout / Official 
Receipt)18. 
2.​ Valid Passport Bio Page of OFW parent / sibling27. 
3.​ PSA Birth Certificate of applicant dependent and PSA Marriage Certificate of parents27. 
4.​ Academic Records: Form 137 / Form 138 / TOR showing GWA >= 80% (Freshmen) or >= 
85% (Upperclassmen) with zero failing marks27. 
5.​ Two (2) pieces 2x2 ID photos with white background and name tag27. 
6.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character27. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum GWA of at least 85.00% (or passing mark specified 
by OWWA RWO) each semester without failing grades27. 
●​ Regular Load: Full credit load per term as prescribed in curriculum18. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing, dropped, or incomplete grades27. 
●​ Return Service: None18. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Inactive OWWA member contribution status18. 
●​ Marriage of scholar during scholarship period (Must remain single)27. 
●​ Dependent age exceeding 21 years old (Freshmen) or 30 years old (Upperclassmen)27. 
●​ Incurring a failing grade or dropping a subject27. 


--- PAGE 44 ---
●​ Concurrent enjoyment of another OWWA or major government scholarship grant27.

OWWA OFW Dependent Scholarship Program (ODSP) 
(ID: 86) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: OFW Dependent Scholarship Program (ODSP)18 
●​ Provider: Overseas Workers Welfare Administration (OWWA)18 
●​ Category: Government / Need-based / OFW Dependent28 
●​ Official Website: https://owwa.gov.ph9 
●​ Application Portal: https://scholarship.owwa.gov.ph27 
●​ Official Guidelines: OWWA ODSP Operating Guidelines18 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The OFW Dependent Scholarship Program offers financial educational assistance to deserving 
dependents of active OWWA members whose monthly basic salary does not exceed USD 
$1,000.00 (or $600.00 basic threshold)18. The program assists lower-income land-based and 
sea-based migrant workers in supporting their dependents through college education in 
Philippine HEIs18. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born Filipino citizen28. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines28. 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Category 1 (Incoming 1st Year Freshmen); Category 2 (2nd to 5th Year 
College Students)18. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No18. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes28. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible18. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in a college or university in the 
Philippines18. 
●​ Academic Requirements: SHS Report Card or college transcript showing General 
Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 75.00% or passing mark with no failing grades18. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 75.00%18. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE28. 


--- PAGE 47 ---
●​ Income Ceilings: OFW parent monthly basic salary must NOT exceed USD $1,000.0018. 
●​ Age Restrictions: Single and not over 21 years old for Category 1 (Freshmen)28; single and 
not over 30 years old for Category 2 (Upperclassmen)28. 
●​ School Restrictions: Philippine-based colleges and universities18. 
●​ Priority Courses: Any undergraduate degree program18. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Dependent child or sibling of an active OWWA member18. 
●​ Good Moral: Required28. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Single marital status mandatory28. OFW membership must be active 
at application18. Allocation capped per province18. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Dependents of low-income active OWWA members entering or enrolled 
in college18. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes18. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes28. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes28. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes28. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No18. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes28. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: July 16 annually28. 
●​ Closing Date: July 31 annually28. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual28. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–202728. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct cash assistance disbursed to scholar28. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual assistance grant28. 
●​ Allowance: Financial assistance of ₱20,000.00 per Academic Year (disbursed at 
₱10,000.00 per semester)28. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into financial assistance28. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into financial assistance28. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: None18. 
●​ Research Allowance: None18. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: None18. 
●​ Return Service: None18.

●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a passing GWA per term (at least 75.00%) with zero failing 
grades18. 
●​ Regular Load: Enrolled in regular load per semester18. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing marks28. 
●​ Return Service: None18.

●​ Inactive OWWA contribution status18. 
●​ Marriage of scholar during award period28. 
●​ Dependent age exceeding 21 years old (Freshmen) or 30 years old (Upperclassmen)28. 
●​ Incurring failing grades28.

OWWA Congressional Migrant Workers Scholarship 
(CMWSP) (ID: 87) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Congressional Migrant Workers Scholarship Program (CMWSP)27 
●​ Provider: Overseas Workers Welfare Administration (OWWA)27 
●​ Category: Government / Merit-and-Need / Science & Technology27 
●​ Official Website: https://owwa.gov.ph9 
●​ Application Portal: https://scholarship.owwa.gov.ph27 
●​ Official Guidelines: Republic Act No. 8042 (Migrant Workers Act); OWWA CMWSP Rules27 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
Enacted under Republic Act No. 8042, the Congressional Migrant Workers Scholarship Program 
provides financial assistance to deserving active or returning OFWs or their legitimate children 
pursuing undergraduate degree programs in Science and Technology priority fields27. The 
program promotes technical skills and scientific advancement among migrant worker 
families27. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born Filipino citizen27. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines27. 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming First-Year College Freshmen)27. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: Yes27. 


--- PAGE 51 ---
●​ Existing College Students?: Ineligible27. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible27. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted as an incoming 1st-year college 
student in an accredited Philippine college or university27. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Senior High School General Weighted Average (GWA) of at 
least 80.00% or equivalent with zero failing grades27. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 80.00%27. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE27. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined family annual income must NOT exceed USD $2,400.0027. 
●​ Age Restrictions: OFW applicant must NOT be older than 45 years on date of 
application27; dependent child applicant must NOT be older than 21 years27. 
●​ School Restrictions: Accredited Philippine colleges and universities27. 
●​ Priority Courses: Restricted to Science and Technology courses based on the 
Department of Science and Technology (DOST) priority list27. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Active or former documented OFW, or a legitimate child of an 
OFW27. 
●​ Good Moral: Required27. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Single marital status required for dependent child applicants27. Must 
pass selection evaluation27. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Incoming 1st-year college freshmen (OFWs under 45 or children of 
OFWs under 21)27. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes (Prior to starting 1st term)27. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: No27. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No27. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No27. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No27. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: No27. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: November 10 annually27. 
●​ Closing Date: November 28 annually27. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual27. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–202727. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct financial grant disbursed to scholar27. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual grant package27. 


--- PAGE 52 ---
●​ Allowance: Financial assistance of ₱60,000.00 per Academic Year (disbursed at 
₱30,000.00 per semester)27. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into annual grant27. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into annual grant27. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: None27. 
●​ Research Allowance: None27. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: None27. 
●​ Return Service: None27. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Proof of OFW Status / Valid Passport Bio Page27. 
2.​ PSA Birth Certificate of dependent applicant27. 
3.​ Proof of Family Income showing combined annual income <= USD $2,400.0027. 
4.​ SHS Form 137 / Form 138 Report Card showing GWA >= 80.00% with zero failing grades27. 
5.​ Certificate of Enrollment / Admission in a DOST priority S&T course27. 
6.​ Two (2) 2x2 ID photos27. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain required GWA (80.00%) per semester without failing grades27. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment in approved S&T degree27. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing marks27. 
●​ Return Service: None27. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Combined family annual income exceeding USD $2,400.0027. 
●​ Enrolling in non-S&T degree programs27. 
●​ Age exceeding 21 years (for dependent) or 45 years (for OFW)27. 
●​ Incurring a failing grade in any subject27.

TESDA Training for Work Scholarship Program (ID: 112) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Training for Work Scholarship Program (TWSP)25 
●​ Provider: Technical Education and Skills Development Authority (TESDA)25 
●​ Category: Government / Technical-Vocational / Sectoral Grant25 
●​ Official Website: https://tesda.gov.ph9 
●​ Application Portal: TESDA Online Program / Regional TESDA Technology Institutions (TTIs) 
and Accredited TVIs25. 
●​ Official Guidelines: TESDA Circular No. 018-2022; TWSP Operating Guidelines25 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Training for Work Scholarship Program delivers direct technical-vocational training 


--- PAGE 55 ---
subsidies in priority growth sectors25. The program expands workforce readiness and technical 
skills for global competitiveness, addressing skill shortages in key industries25. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen25. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines25. 
●​ Education Level: Technical-Vocational Education and Training (TVET)9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Non-degree short-term TVET qualifications (NC I to NC IV)25. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No25. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Eligible (Provided not currently enrolled in another TESDA 
scholarship)25. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Eligible25. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in a TESDA-registered TVET 
program25. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Basic literacy and numeracy; satisfies specific TVET 
qualification entry standards25. 
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Passing basic qualification entry 
test required)9. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE9. 
●​ Age Restrictions: Must be at least 15 years old at the start of the training program25. 
●​ School Restrictions: TESDA Technology Institutions (TTIs) and TESDA-accredited private 
TVIs25. 
●​ Priority Courses: TESDA priority sector qualifications (Construction, IT-BPM, Tourism, 
Agriculture, Logistics, Manufacturing)25. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Unemployed workers, underemployed, returning OFWs, 
displaced workers prioritized25. 
●​ Good Moral: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Health Requirements: Physically fit to undergo technical training25. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Trainee must NOT be currently enrolled in any other active TESDA 
scholarship grant25. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Any Filipino citizen aged 15 or older seeking technical skills training25. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes25. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes25. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes25. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes25. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: Yes25. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (For a different NC level or qualification sector)25. 


--- PAGE 56 ---
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Year-round / Rolling intake9. 
●​ Closing Date: December 31 annually (or subject to allocation batch schedules)9. 
●​ Application Cycle: Rolling / Continuous9. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–20279. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: 100% Full training cost waiver paid to TVI25. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into daily training allowance25. 
●​ Allowance: Daily training allowance (₱160.00 per attendance day)25. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into training materials25. 
●​ Transportation: None25. 
●​ Insurance: Accident insurance coverage provided during training25. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: Free National Competency Assessment (Assessment Fee 
covered)25. 
●​ Research Allowance: None25. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: None25. 
●​ Return Service: None9. 
●​ Total Financial Package Value: Estimated at ₱15,000.00 per qualification course9. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Accomplished TESDA Learner's Profile Form (MIS 03-02)25. 
2.​ Birth Certificate (PSA or Local Civil Registrar) or PhilSys ID25. 
3.​ Barangay Clearance or Police Clearance25. 
4.​ Three (3) 1x1 ID photos (white background, shirt with collar)25. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain 80% minimum attendance rate and pass practical competency 
evaluations25. 
●​ Regular Load: Full attendance during training schedule25. 
●​ Return Service: None9. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Unexcused absences exceeding 20% of total training hours25. 
●​ Simultaneous enrollment in another active TESDA scholarship program25. 
●​ Age below 15 years old25.

TESDA Special Training for Employment Program (ID:

●​ Official Website: https://tesda.gov.ph9

●​ Official Guidelines: TESDA Circular No. 018-2022; STEP Operating Guidelines25 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Special Training for Employment Program delivers community-based specialty skills

training to informal sector workers, displaced personnel, and underprivileged citizens25. STEP

3. Official Eligibility Requirements

OWWA Education and Livelihood Assistance Program 
(ELAP) (ID: 124) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Education and Livelihood Assistance Program (ELAP)9 
●​ Provider: Overseas Workers Welfare Administration (OWWA)9 
●​ Category: Government / Need-and-Crisis / OFW Dependent31 
●​ Official Website: 
https://owwa.gov.ph/education-and-livelihood-assistance-program-elap/9 
●​ Application Portal: OWWA Regional Welfare Offices (RWOs)27. 
●​ Official Guidelines: OWWA ELAP Operating Guidelines; Republic Act No. 804218 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Education and Livelihood Assistance Program provides educational grants to surviving 
dependents of deceased or permanently incapacitated active OWWA members18. In addition 
to educational grants across primary, secondary, and tertiary levels, ELAP grants a one-time 


--- PAGE 63 ---
livelihood grant to the surviving spouse or parent, providing financial support to families 
affected by migrant worker casualties31. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born Filipino citizen18. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines18. 
●​ Education Level: Elementary, Secondary (JHS/SHS), College / Tertiary9. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: All year levels from primary through tertiary education9. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No9. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes9. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible18. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled in a recognized elementary, secondary, or 
tertiary institution18. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Passing General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 75.00% 
or passing mark9. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 75.00%9. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined family annual gross income must NOT exceed ₱250,000.009. 
●​ Age Restrictions: Dependent child must be below 21 years old for college level (or under 
18 for basic education)18. 
●​ School Restrictions: Recognized public or private educational institutions18. 
●​ Priority Courses: Any course or grade level18. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be a surviving dependent child of an active OWWA member 
who died or suffered permanent total disability31. Limited to ONE (1) child beneficiary per 
deceased/incapacitated OFW family31. 
●​ Good Moral: Required18. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: OFW parent must have been an active OWWA member at the time 
of death or permanent disability31. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Surviving child of deceased or permanently incapacitated active OFW31. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes9. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No18. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (Continuous annual support until course 
completion)18. 


--- PAGE 64 ---
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Year-round intake following casualty event9. 
●​ Closing Date: Specified per regional office window / annual cycle9. 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual9. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–20279. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct cash assistance grant18. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual grant package18. 
●​ Educational Allowance (by level): 
○​ Primary Education: ₱5,000.00 per school year18. 
○​ Secondary Education (JHS/SHS): ₱8,000.00 per school year18. 
○​ Tertiary Education (College): ₱10,000.00 to ₱15,000.00 per school year9. 
●​ Livelihood Support: One-time livelihood grant of ₱15,000.00 awarded to the surviving 
spouse (if married) or parent (if single) of the deceased/incapacitated OFW31. 
●​ Total Financial Award Value: Up to ₱15,000.00/year education grant + ₱15,000.00 
livelihood grant9. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Official Death Certificate or Medical Certificate of Permanent Disability of OFW issued 
by proper authority18. 
2.​ Proof of Active OWWA Membership at time of casualty event30. 
3.​ PSA Marriage Certificate of parents and PSA Birth Certificate of child beneficiary18. 
4.​ Form 138 Report Card or College Transcript showing passing GWA >= 75.00%9. 
5.​ BIR Tax Exemption Certificate, ITR, or Barangay Certificate of Indigency (Income <= 
₱250,000.00)9. 
6.​ Certificate of Enrollment from school18. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain passing GWA (at least 75.00%) every school year9. 
●​ Regular Load: Continuous enrollment in regular grade/year level18. 
●​ No Failures: Passing all enrolled subjects18. 
●​ Return Service: None9. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ OFW parent not an active OWWA member at time of death or disability31. 
●​ More than one child beneficiary applying from the same family (Strictly 1 child per family 
rule)31. 
●​ Combined family income exceeding ₱250,000.009. 


--- PAGE 65 ---
●​ Academic failure or dropping out from school18.

user.parent_ofw_status IN ('Deceased', 'Permanently_Incapacitated') AND 
user.is_sole_family_elap_applicant == true31. Failing to validate these parameters will 
cause active OFW families to apply incorrectly31.

System Migration Directives and Database SQL 
To resolve data corruptions, missing sectoral parameters, and miscalculated grant amounts 
identified during canonical audits across these 15 programs, database administrators must 
execute the following structural alteration script and data updates on the live database 
(public.scholarships). 
Schema Alterations (DDL) 
 
 
 
SQL 
-- Extend public.scholarships with strict structural and sectoral constraint columns​
ALTER TABLE public.scholarships​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS eligible_year_levels INT[] DEFAULT '{1}',​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS incoming_year_only BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS requires_current_enrollment BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS renewal_gwa NUMERIC(5,2) DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS age_limit_min INT DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS age_limit_max INT DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS parent_employment_restriction VARCHAR(150) DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS sectoral_restriction VARCHAR(150) DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS is_emergency_grant BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS includes_starter_toolkits BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS one_beneficiary_per_family BOOLEAN DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS return_service_required BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;​

Recommendation Engine Architecture 
The completion of canonical audits across these Group B scholarship programs establishes a 
verified data foundation for the ISKONNECT scholarship matching platform1. By replacing 
unverified aggregator claims with official primary agency mandates, the system prevents 
misdirection and ensures accurate recommendations for Filipino students1. 


--- PAGE 71 ---
The recommendation engine processes student profiles through a multi-stage deterministic 
pipeline1: 
The initial stage conducts hard temporal and level filtering1. It verifies whether a student's 
education level and year level match scholarship.eligible_year_levels, enforces zero earned 
college units when scholarship.incoming_year_only is true (e.g., BPMSP TVET ID 77, OWWA 
CMWSP ID 87)21, and validates age restrictions (e.g., age under 21 for AFPEBSO ID 56)17. 
The second stage evaluates quantitative eligibility criteria1. It checks whether user.gwa >= 
scholarship.minimum_gwa (enforcing 80% for NCIP EAP ID 52 versus 85% for NCIP MBSP ID 
53)14, confirms user.family_income <= scholarship.income_limit (e.g., ₱200,000 for DA ACEF ID 
55 versus ₱2,000,000 for BPMSP ID 77)9, and validates specific salary caps (e.g., OFW parent 
monthly salary under USD $1,000 for OWWA ODSP ID 86)28. 
The third stage validates sectoral requirements, affiliation status, and institutional parameters1. 
It confirms tribal certifications (user.has_ncip_coc == true for NCIP ID 52 and 53)10, validates 
RSBSA agricultural registry status (user.is_rsbsa_dependent == true for DA ACEF ID 55)16, 
verifies active OWWA membership and casualty flags (user.parent_ofw_status IN ('Deceased', 
'Incapacitated') for OWWA ELAP ID 124)31, and restricts institutional grants (such as AFPEBSO ID 
56) to SUCs17. 
Implementing these database updates and pipeline constraints aligns the live ISKONNECT 
database with canonical provider rules, eliminating matching errors and delivering reliable 
scholarship recommendations1.

--- PAGE 1 ---
Canonical Verification Report: Group B 
Private Foundation Scholarships for the 
ISKONNECT Matching Engine 
Executive Summary & Verification Protocol 
The ISKONNECT scholarship matching platform operates an automated recommendation 
engine designed to process student profile vectors against higher education financial assistance 
programs across public, institutional, and private sectors in the Philippines. To eliminate 
false-positive recommendations that direct applicants to ineligible programs and false-negative 
omissions that suppress viable funding, this report establishes the canonical verification ground 
truth for all Group B database records that have not yet undergone canonical auditing. 
Enterprise database updates require deterministic verification: every eligibility parameter must 
be validated against Tier 1 primary evidence, mapped to explicit SQL data types, and reconciled 
with governing agency mandates. Where an official provider document or governing policy does 
not explicitly state a requirement, platform protocol mandates designating the field as NOT 
SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE rather than introducing unverified assumptions. 
Data verification strictly follows a four-tiered authority hierarchy: 
●​ Tier 1 (Highest Authority): Direct government agency web portals (.gov.ph), official 
provider portals, downloadable policy guidelines, CHED Memorandum Orders (CMOs), 
GSIS Policy and Procedural Guidelines (PPGs), DOST Administrative Orders, and official 
application portals. 
●​ Tier 2 (Official Announcements): Press releases, downloadable application instructions, 
official FAQs, and direct advisories published by scholarship secretariats. 
●​ Tier 3 (Trusted Secondary Sources): University scholarship office notices and 
recognized educational registries used exclusively to locate primary documentation. Tier 3 
data cannot override Tier 1 or Tier 2 mandates. 
●​ Tier 4 (Unverified Sources): Blogs, student forums, social media aggregators, and 
unverified summaries. These sources are permanently excluded from live database 
updates. 
Per platform protocol, scholarship programs that have already received complete canonical 
verification reports (e.g., DOST-SEI Undergraduate and JLSS, CHED BPMSP, CHED CMSP, 
CHED MSRS, Megaworld Foundation, SM Foundation, GSIS GSSP, CHED CoScho, and 
Quezon City Scholarship Program) are assigned to Group A and are excluded from re-research. 
This report presents exhaustive canonical audits for all seven target private foundation 
scholarships under Group B. 
Ayala Foundation U-Go Scholar Grant (ID: 11) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Ayala Foundation U-Go Scholar Grant (U-GO Scholar Grant) 
●​ Provider: Ayala Foundation, Inc. (in partnership with U-Go Global) 


--- PAGE 2 ---
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need / Female Empowerment 
●​ Official Website: https://ayalafoundation.org/programs/scholarships/ 
●​ Application Portal: Official Eligibility Form Portal (sent via email/QR upon eligibility 
screening) 
●​ Official Guidelines: Ayala Foundation & U-Go Grant Program Official Guidelines 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Ayala Foundation U-Go Scholar Grant provides financial assistance and educational 
support to high-potential young women from low-income families enrolled in public or state 
universities and colleges in the Philippines, helping cover living, transportation, and school 
expenses to ensure college completion and promote gender equity in higher education. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st Year, 2nd Year, 3rd Year (and 4th Year if enrolled in a 5-year 
degree program) 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or will enroll in a public or state 
university/college (SUC/LUC) in the Philippines 
●​ Academic Requirements: Minimum Grade Point Average / General Weighted Average 
(GWA) of at least 85% with no failing grades; no disciplinary or administrative cases 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Demonstrated financial need (family income within low-income 
threshold, parent ITR, BIR Tax Exemption, or Indigency) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted to public or state universities and colleges (SUCs/LUCs) in 
the Philippines 
●​ Priority Courses: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be a female student 
●​ Good Moral: Required (must have no disciplinary or administrative cases) 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Must NOT have any existing scholarship grant 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Female Filipino incoming 1st-year, 2nd-year, or 3rd-year college 
students (and 4th-year students taking a 5-year course) enrolled in public or state 
universities. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 


--- PAGE 3 ---
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes (Only if taking a 5-year course and entering 4th year; 
strictly ineligible if expecting to graduate in the current AY). 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes, provided they meet all eligibility criteria and hold 
no other scholarship grants. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: May 5, 2026 
●​ Closing Date: June 6, 2026 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Public/state universities are covered 
under Republic Act 10931; grant provides direct educational financial support). 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual financial assistance. 
●​ Allowance: Approximately PHP 40,000.00 annual financial assistance. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into annual grant. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into annual grant. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
●​ Other Benefits: Leadership development programs, professional development training, 
mentoring, and scholar support network access. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Duly accomplished online application form. 
2.​ Current official Certificate of Registration / Enrollment signed by the registrar with wet 
signature. 
3.​ Latest copy of grades / Transcript of Records signed by the registrar with wet signature. 
4.​ Proof of financial need (Parents' or guardians' most recent Income Tax Return [ITR], BIR 
Tax Exemption Certificate, Barangay Certificate of Indigency, or OFW/seafarer 
contract/proof of income). 
5.​ Proof of college admission or Senior High School diploma (for incoming 1st-year 
students). 
6.​ Recent copy of electric or water bill (if available). 
7.​ Official Recommendation Letter (for shortlisted applicants). 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum GWA of 85.00% or equivalent each academic term. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit load per term in a public/state university. 


--- PAGE 4 ---
●​ No Failures: Zero failing grades. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
### 9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Male gender. 
●​ Possession or active enjoyment of any other scholarship grant. 
●​ Enrollment in a private higher education institution. 
●​ Students expecting to graduate during the current academic year. 
●​ GWA below 85.00% or presence of failing/incomplete grades. 
●​ History of administrative or disciplinary sanctions. 
10. Temporal Eligibility Matrix

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Gender Mismatch: Live database state currently lacks a gender filter tag. Displaying ID 11 
to male applicants creates false positives. 
●​ Private School False Positives: System must filter user.school_type == 'SUC' OR 'LUC'. 
Enrolled private university students will be rejected immediately. 
●​ Dual Grant Violation: Automated matching engine must verify 
user.has_existing_scholarsh[span_113](start_span)[span_113](end_span)ip == false.

Metrobank Foundation ACCESS Program (ID: 13) 


--- PAGE 6 ---
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Assistance for the Completion of College Education for Superior Students 
(MBFI-ACCESS) Program 
●​ Provider: Metrobank Foundation, Inc. (MBFI) / GT Foundation, Inc. (GTFI) 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need 
●​ Official Website: https://www.mbfoundation.org.ph 
●​ Application Portal: Coordinated directly through scholarship offices of designated partner 
universities and Metrobank Foundation offices 
●​ Official Guidelines: MBFI-ACCESS Scholarship Program Guidelines & Partner University 
MOAs 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Metrobank Foundation ACCESS Program bridges the gap between financial limitations and 
academic talent by granting four-year higher education scholarships to academically gifted 
college students in priority disciplines across designated partner universities nationwide. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming Freshmen) and Year 2 (Sophomores in 
Engineering tracks) 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No (Incoming Freshmen for general priority courses; 
Sophomores for Engineering). 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (Incoming 2nd-year Engineering students in partner 
HEIs). 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Accepted or enrolled in a priority course at an MBFI 
partner university 
●​ Academic Requirements: General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 85.00% or 
equivalent in High School / previous college term; passing score in MBFI qualification 
exams and interviews 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined gross annual family income not exceeding PHP 500,000.00 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to MBFI partner universities/colleges (CHED 
Centers of Excellence/Development or Level II/III accredited institutions) 
●​ Priority Courses: Specialized Science & Math, Teacher Education (BEED/BSED), 
Information Technology, Engineering, Business Administration / Entrepreneurship, 
Accountancy, Nursing, Architecture, Statistics 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Good Moral: Required 


--- PAGE 7 ---
●​ Health Requirements: Physically and mentally fit - Other Official Rules: Must pass 
screening, written examinations, and panel evaluation processes 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Incoming college freshmen enrolled in priority courses, and incoming 
sophomores taking Engineering at accredited partner institutions. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes (as incoming 1st-year students entering college or 
entering 2nd-year Engineering). 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes (Incoming 2nd-year Engineering students). 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: No 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced per academic year cycle via partner university scholarship 
offices 
●​ Closing Date: Specified in annual partner university advisories 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Full tuition and matriculation fee coverage at partner universities. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Direct monthly living allowance. 
●​ Allowance: Fixed semester living and book allowances. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into semester financial package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: Monetary cash incentives for Latin Honor graduates (e.g., PHP 
10,000 for Magna Cum Laude, PHP 5,000 for Cum Laude). 
●​ Return Service: None (Encouraged participation in "Pay-it-forward Service to the 4Cs" via 
ASSET alumni association). 
●​ Other Benefits: Holistic formation programs, leadership seminars, workshops, and job 
placement assistance post-graduation. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Accomplished MBFI-ACCESS Application Form. 
2.​ Official Report Card / Transcript of Records / Form 137 showing minimum GWA of 
85.00%. 
3.​ Parents' Income Tax Return (ITR) or BIR Certificate of Tax Exemption showing annual 
family income below PHP 500,000.00. 
4.​ Certificate of Enrollment / Notice of Admission from an MBFI partner university. 


--- PAGE 8 ---
5.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
6.​ Medical Certificate confirming physical and mental fitness. 
7.​ 2x2 ID Pictures. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum semester GWA of 85.00% or university passing 
standards. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment per term in approved priority course. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing grades in enrolled subjects. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Enrolling in non-partner higher education institutions. 
●​ Combined annual family income exceeding PHP 500,000.00. 
●​ GWA dropping below 85.00% or presence of failing grades. 
●​ Failure to pass MBFI qualification examination or interview evaluation.

BPI Foundation Pagpupugay Scholarship (ID: 14) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: BPI Foundation Pagpupugay Scholarship Program 
●​ Provider: BPI Foundation, Inc. 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need / Frontliner Next-of-Kin 
●​ Official Website: 
https://www.bpifoundation.org/programs/special-projects/pagpupugay-scholarship 
●​ Application Portal: Online submission via BPI Foundation portal forms or partner 
university scholarship offices 
●​ Official Guidelines: BPI Foundation Pagpupugay Scholarship Guidelines & Application 
E-Forms 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The BPI Foundation Pagpupugay Scholarship honors the heroic contributions of medical 
frontliners who contracted COVID-19 or passed away in the line of duty during the pandemic by 
providing full financial support for the tertiary education of their qualified next of kin. 
### 3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st Year, 2nd Year, 3rd Year, 4th Year, and 5th Year 


--- PAGE 11 ---
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or applying to any 4-year or 5-year 
college/university program in BPI Foundation partner schools nationwide (or non-partner 
HEIs upon direct coordination) 
●​ Academic Requirements: General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 85.00% or 
equivalent / prevailing university standards for the previous school year (incoming 1st 
year) or previous semester (incoming 2nd-5th year) 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Demonstrated financial need verified via parents'/guardian's Income Tax 
Return (ITR) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Open to students in BPI Foundation partner universities (e.g., Ateneo 
de Manila, Mapua, Malayan Colleges, National Teachers College, University of Nueva 
Caceres) and accredited nationwide HEIs 
●​ Priority Courses: Any 4-year or 5-year undergraduate degree program 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be a qualified next-of-kin of medical frontliners (doctors, 
nurses, medical technologists, community health workers, administrative/utility/support 
staff in healthcare facilities) who passed away or contracted COVID-19 in the line of duty. 
Priority order: children of married frontliners; next-of-kin up to 3rd degree consanguinity 
for single frontliners 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character) 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Requires hospital endorsement letter, official affidavits of 
endorsement and no objection, and proof of frontliner COVID-19 medical/death status 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Qualified next-of-kin of eligible medical frontliners entering or currently 
enrolled in 1st to 5th year of college. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes (4th and 5th year undergraduate students). 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (Scholarships are evaluated for annual renewal). 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced per annual cycle (typically Q2/Q3) 
●​ Closing Date: Announced annually (e.g., July 31 / extended to September) 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 


--- PAGE 12 ---
●​ Tuition: Up to PHP 100,000.00 per academic year covering tuition and matriculation fees 
(paid directly to partner HEI). 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Provided as monthly living subsidy for State University/College (SUC) 
scholars. 
●​ Allowance: Learning assistance allowance provided for SUC scholars. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into learning assistance allowance. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
●​ Other Benefits: Annual renewal support through course completion. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Fully accomplished Scholarship Application Form and Data Privacy Form. 
2.​ Endorsement Letter signed by authorized representative of hospital/healthcare facility 
where frontliner was assigned. 
3.​ Affidavit of Endorsement and No Objection (from frontliner if living, or next-of-kin if 
deceased). 
4.​ PSA Birth Certificate of applicant (and family birth certificates showing common parents if 
nephew/niece up to 3rd degree). 
5.​ PSA Marriage Certificate (for widow/widower or parents). 
6.​ PRC ID or License ID of the medical frontliner. 
7.​ Death Certificate of frontliner indicating COVID-19 cause of death OR Medical Certificate 
indicating COVID-19 infection. 
8.​ Parents' or guardian's latest Income Tax Return (ITR). 
9.​ Official Transcript of Records / Copy of Grades showing minimum GWA of 85.00%. 
10.​Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum GWA of 85.00% or prevailing university standards 
each semester. 
●​ Regular Load: Enrolled in full term credit load. 
●​ No Failures: Compliance with university retention policies. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Applicant is not a qualified next-of-kin (child or up to 3rd degree consanguinity) of an 
eligible COVID-19 medical frontliner. 
●​ Frontliner was not assigned to a hospital or recognized healthcare facility. 
●​ Semester GWA dropping below 85.00%. 
●​ Failure to submit mandatory hospital endorsements or affidavits. 


--- PAGE 13 ---

San Miguel Foundation Scholarship (ID: 15) 
1. Scholarship Identity 


--- PAGE 15 ---
●​ Official Name: San Miguel Foundation Educational Assistance / Community Scholarship 
Program

subsidiaries 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Need-and-Merit / Host Community Support 
●​ Official Website: https://www.sanmiguel.com.ph/page/san-miguel-foundation 
●​ Application Portal: Processed via scholarship offices of partner universities and local SMC 
plant community relations offices 
●​ Official Guidelines: San Miguel Foundation Community & College Scholarship Program 
Guidelines 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The San Miguel Foundation Scholarship provides higher education access and financial 
assistance to academically deserving but underprivileged students, particularly from San Miguel 
Corporation host communities and partner universities, in degree courses aligned with national 
infrastructure, industrial development, and corporate talent needs. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: Preference given to residents of San Miguel Corporation host communities or 
operational areas 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate (and Technical-Vocational courses) 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st Year, 2nd Year, 3rd Year, 4th Year, and 5th Year 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in designated partner universities 
or SUCs/LUCs in SMC host communities 
●​ Academic Requirements: Academically deserving student (minimum GWA parameter 
governed by partner university agreements, typically 85.00% to 88.00% without failing 
grades) 
●​ Minimum GWA: 88.00% (Live database baseline; partner HEI MOA standards apply). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Financially challenged background (family annual income < PHP 
400,000.00 / demonstrated indigency) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted to SMC partner universities (e.g., UP Diliman, Dualtech 
Center, partner HEIs near SMC plants/townships) 
●​ Priority Courses: Engineering (Civil, Electrical, Mechanical, Chemical), Agriculture, 
Agribusiness, Business / Accountancy, Applied Sciences (Applied Physics, Molecular 
Biology & Biotechnology), Technical-Vocational skills 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Residents of SMC host communities / underprivileged family 
dependents 
●​ Good Moral: Required 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE

university scholarship committee

PLDT-Smart Foundation Gabay Guro Scholarship (ID: 
16) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: PLDT-Smart Foundation Gabay Guro Scholarship Program 
●​ Provider: PLDT-Smart Foundation (PSF) & PLDT Manager's Club, Inc. 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need / Teacher Education 
●​ Official Website: https://www.gabayguro.com 
●​ Application Portal: Administered directly through scholarship offices of partner State 
Universities and Colleges (SUCs) nationwide 


--- PAGE 19 ---
●​ Official Guidelines: Gabay Guro Scholarship Questionnaire & Application Form (Revised 
2023) 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The PLDT-Smart Foundation Gabay Guro Scholarship empowers aspiring educators by 
providing full financial support to academically gifted but underprivileged students pursuing 
Bachelor of Education degrees, expanding access to quality Teacher Education and improving 
K-12 instruction across the Philippines. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate

●​ Academic Requirements: General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 85.00% during the

●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined annual family gross income must NOT exceed PHP 
250,000.00 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to Gabay Guro partner SUCs nationwide (over 40+ 
partner state universities including MSU Gensan, PNU, etc.) 
●​ Priority Courses: Bachelor of Elementary Education (BEED), Bachelor of Secondary 
Education (BSED) with majors in English, Mathematics, or Science 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Aspiring teachers / Teacher Education majors 
●​ Good Moral: Required 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE

Gokongwei Brothers Foundation STEM-College 
Scholarship (ID: 72)

1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: GBF STEM-College Scholarship (formerly GBF-Gokongwei Group STEM 
Scholarship for Excellence) 
●​ Provider: Gokongwei Brothers Foundation, Inc. (GBF) in partnership with Gokongwei 
Group business units 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need / STEM Focus 
●​ Official Website: https://www.gokongweibrothersfoundation.org 
●​ Application Portal: GBF Online Portal (bit.ly/GBFSTEMCollege) 
●​ Official Guidelines: GBF STEM-College Scholarship Program Guidelines AY 2026–2027 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The GBF STEM-College Scholarship supports outstanding, financially challenged Filipino 
college students pursuing priority undergraduate STEM degrees, equipping them with financial 
aid, leadership training, digital upskilling, and career opportunities within the Gokongwei Group. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate - Eligible Year Levels: 1st Year (Incoming 
Freshmen), 2nd Year, 3rd Year, 4th Year, and 5th Year (Continuing Students) 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (2nd Year and above) 
●​ Graduate Students?: No (Applied strictly to first Bachelor's degree; GBF TeachSTEM 
Masters is a separate graduate track). 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or planning to enroll in a priority STEM degree 
program at a Philippine university/college 
●​ Academic Requirements: General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 85.00% (or 2.0 / 
equivalent) with zero failed, dropped, or incomplete grades in high school or college; 
incoming freshmen must belong to the Top 10% of their SHS graduating batch 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% (or 2.0 / equivalent) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: Top 10% of Senior High School graduating batch (mandatory 
requirement for incoming freshmen) 
●​ Income Ceilings: Demonstrated financial need (submission of 2025 ITR, Certificate of 
Employment with salary, or BIR Tax Exemption required) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Open to public and private universities in the Philippines offering GBF 
priority STEM degree programs 
●​ Priority Courses: GBF-identified priority STEM degree courses (Engineering, Information 
Technology, Computer Science, Data Science, Chemistry, Life Sciences, Applied 
Mathematics, Agriculture, Food Technology) 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: STEM degree students 
●​ Good Moral: Required (good moral standing with active extracurricular/community 
involvement) 


--- PAGE 24 ---
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Must be pursuing first bachelor's degree; willing to fulfill return 
service obligation 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Incoming college freshmen (Top 10% batch rank) and continuing college 
students (2nd year and above) enrolled in priority STEM courses. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes (4th and 5th year continuing STEM students). 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Q1/Q2 annually 
●​ Closing Date: May 31, 2026 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct annual financial grant ranging from PHP 80,000.00 to PHP 120,000.00 
(credited directly to scholar's bank account to cover tuition and academic fees). 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into annual financial grant. 
●​ Allowance: Integrated into annual financial grant. - Book Allowance: Integrated into annual 
financial grant. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. - Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED 
IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: Mandatory return service obligation / commitment to work within 
Gokongwei Group companies or local STEM industries. 
●​ Other Benefits: Leadership development programs, upskilling workshops, on-the-job 
training/internships, and career placement opportunities within the Gokongwei Group. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Fully accomplished online application form. 
2.​ For Incoming Freshmen: Certified True Copy of Grade 12 Report Card (Form 138 / Form 
137), Certificate of Batch Ranking showing Top 10% rank in Grade 12 (or Grade 11), 
Notice of Admission / Proof of University Application. 
3.​ For Continuing Students: Certified True Copy of Grades for the last 2 consecutive 
semesters, Registration Form for current term. 
4.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character.

OFW employment contract, Grab/Lalamove earnings record, or BIR Tax Exemption 
Certificate).

8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum GWA of 85.00% (2.0 or equivalent) each academic 
term. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment in priority STEM course 
●​ No Failures: Zero failed, incomplete, or dropped grades 
●​ Return Service: Compliance with return service commitment and participation in scholar 
development sessions. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Enrolling in non-STEM degree programs 
●​ GWA dropping below 85.00% (2.0) or presence of failed, dropped, or incomplete grades 
●​ Incoming freshmen failing to prove Top 10% batch ranking 
●​ Application for second undergraduate degree

Aboitiz Brights (Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship) 
(ID: 75) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship Program (Aboitiz Brights) 
●​ Provider: Aboitiz Foundation, Inc. 
●​ Category: Private / Foundation / Merit-and-Need / Leadership Development 
●​ Official Website: https://aboitiz.com/aboitiz-foundation/aboitiz-future-leaders-scholarship 
●​ Application Portal: Submitted through partner university scholarship offices via online 
application portal link 
●​ Official Guidelines: Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship Program Guidelines & FAQ 
●​ Current Status: Active 
### 2. Purpose The Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship empowers talented young Filipinos 
demonstrating exceptional leadership potential by providing full tertiary education financial 
support, mentorship, upskilling, and professional development to prepare them as future 
industry leaders. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: Nationwide (open to eligible students nationwide enrolled in partner schools) 
●​ Education Level: College / Undergraduate 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 2 ONLY (Incoming Sophomore Students) 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No (Incoming Freshmen are strictly barred: "The scholarship 
is ONLY open to incoming sophomore students who have completed their first year in 
college"). 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (Exclusively incoming 2nd-year / sophomore students). 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled in an identified priority degree program at an 
Aboitiz Foundation partner university 
●​ Academic Requirements: Completed 1st year in college with strong academic 
performance (minimum GWA specified by partner university / foundation rules, typically 
88.00% or 2.0 equivalent without failing grades) 


--- PAGE 28 ---
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Live database state displays 
75; official policy mandates maintaining good academic standing per partner university 
criteria). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Financial need evaluated, but 
no rigid income cap published) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to Aboitiz Foundation partner universities (e.g., 
PSAU, UP System, DLSU, UST, Ateneo de Manila) 
●​ Priority Courses: Pre-identified degree programs aligned with Aboitiz Group business 
units (Engineering, Information Technology, Data Science, Agriculture, Agribusiness, 
Veterinary Medicine, Finance / Business Administration) 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Student leaders / high leadership potential 
●​ Good Moral: Required (no record of any form of disciplinary action) 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Current recipients of scholarships from other corporate foundations 
are NOT eligible under the program 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Incoming sophomore students (Year 2) who have completed their 1st 
year of college at a partner university. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: No (Incoming freshmen cannot apply; must complete 1st 
year first). - Can current sophomores apply?: Yes (Primary target cohort entering 2nd 
year). 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: No 
### 5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Summer period preceding 2nd year intake 
●​ Closing Date: Announced annually via university scholarship offices 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Full tuition fee coverage. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Direct monthly living allowance. 
●​ Allowance: Integrated into monthly living allowance. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into scholarship package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: Board Topnotcher Monetary Incentive. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: Latin Honor Monetary Incentive and Academic Excellence 
Incentive. 
●​ Return Service: No post-graduation employment return service required. 


--- PAGE 29 ---
●​ Other Benefits: Learning & leadership development sessions, mentorship, and mandatory 
400-hour internship within Aboitiz Group business units. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Copy of Student ID. 
2.​ Copy of Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
3.​ Certified Copy of College Grades starting 1st year / Transcript of Records. 4. Proof of 
Enrollment in an approved priority course at a partner university. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain good academic standing each semester per university 
standards. 
●​ Regular Load: Continuous full-time enrollment in priority course. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing grades or disciplinary records. - Return Service: Completion of 
mandatory 400-hour Aboitiz Group internship and active participation in foundation 
events. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Incoming freshmen, 3rd year, or 4th year students. 
●​ Active enjoyment of a scholarship from another corporate foundation. 
●​ Enrollment in a non-partner university or non-priority degree program. 
●​ Presence of disciplinary records or failing grades.

Synthesis of Database Mutations & System 
Architecture 
To ensure operational integrity and eliminate false recommendations across the ISKONNECT 
matching platform, database administrators must execute the DDL schema additions and DML 
data updates detailed below. 
Database Schema Alterations (DDL) 
-- Extend public.scholarships with temporal, sectoral, and 
institutional control columns​
ALTER TABLE public.scholarships​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS eligible_year_levels INT[] DEFAULT '{1}',​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS incoming_year_only BOOLEAN DEFAULT true,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS requires_current_enrollment BOOLEAN DEFAULT 
false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS gender_restriction VARCHAR(20) DEFAULT NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS sectoral_restriction VARCHAR(100) DEFAULT 
NULL,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS partner_school_restricted BOOLEAN DEFAULT 
false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS scholarship_exclusivity_clause BOOLEAN 
DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS corporate_grant_exclusivity_clause BOOLEAN 
DEFAULT false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS return_service_required BOOLEAN DEFAULT 
false,​
ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS needs_manual_review BOOLEAN DEFAULT false;​
 
Data Mutation Script (DML) 
-- 1. Update Ayala Foundation U-Go Scholar Grant (ID 11)​
UPDATE public.scholarships​

  incoming_year_only = false,​
  requires_current_enrollment = true,​

Multi-Stage Engine Recommendation Pipeline Architecture 
The automated recommendation algorithm processes applicant vectors through three sequential 
filtering stages to guarantee data compliance: 
1.​ Stage 1: Hard Level, Temporal, and Gender Filtering 
○​ Verifies whether user.year_level is contained within 
scholarship.eligible_year_levels. 
○​ Enforces user.earned_college_units == 0 whenever 
scholarship.incoming_year_only is set to true. 
○​ Validates strict demographic and intake constraints, such as verifying user.gender 
== 'FEMALE' for the Ayala Foundation U-Go Grant (ID 11) or restricting entry strictly 
to user.year_level == 2 for the Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship (ID 75). 
2.​ Stage 2: Quantitative and Socioeconomic Eligibility Bounds 
○​ Evaluates user.gwa >= scholarship.minimum_gwa or checks whether 
user.class_rank <= scholarship.rank_cutoff_alternative (such as the Top 10% batch 
rank required for freshman applicants under GBF STEM ID 72). 
○​ Confirms user.family_income <= scholarship.income_limit (such as enforcing the 
PHP 250,000.00 ceiling for Gabay Guro ID 16 or PHP 500,000.00 for MBFI 
ACCESS ID 13). 
○​ Enforces program exclusivity rules by validating user.has_existing_scholarship == 
false for Ayala U-Go (ID 11) and user.has_corporate_foundation_grant == false for 
Aboitiz Brights (ID 75). 
3.​ Stage 3: Institutional, Sectoral, and Course Validation 
○​ Matches user.school_id against the partner_schools database table whenever 
scholarship.partner_school_restricted is set to true. 
○​ Verifies user.course_code against approved priority degree arrays (such as 
restricting Gabay Guro ID 16 strictly to BEED and BSED majors, or matching STEM 
disciplines for GBF ID 72). 
○​ Validates specific sectoral attributes, such as verifying next-of-kin documentation for 
COVID-19 medical frontliners under the BPI Foundation Pagpupugay Scholarship 
(ID 14). 
Executing these database updates and architectural pipeline enhancements aligns the live 
ISKONNECT scholarship database with canonical provider rules, eliminating false 
recommendations and providing Filipino students with reliable scholarship matching results.

Security Bank Foundation State Universities 
Scholarship (ID: 58) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Security Bank Foundation State Universities Scholarship (Scholars for 
Better Communities Program) 
●​ Provider: Security Bank Foundation, Inc. (SBFI) 
●​ Category: Private / Corporate Foundation / Merit-and-Need 
●​ Official Website: https://www.securitybank.com/foundation 
●​ Application Portal: https://www.securitybank.com/foundation 
●​ Official Guidelines: SBFI Scholars for Better Communities Guidelines 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Security Bank Foundation State Universities Scholarship is a corporate financial assistance 
and human capital development initiative established to support academically talented students 
entering designated public higher education institutions. By financing tertiary degrees in 
disciplines aligned with corporate operations, the foundation aims to expand access to quality 
education, build leadership capacity within state universities, and provide direct career pathways 
in the banking and finance sector. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming Freshmen) and limited upperclassmen in specific 
partner SUCs. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: Primary focus is incoming freshmen; limited upperclassmen 
entry exists per SUC slot allocation. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (limited slots at specific partner State Universities and 
Colleges). 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be accepted or enrolled in a partner State 
University or College, such as Polytechnic University of the Philippines (PUP). 
●​ Academic Requirements: Must possess a Grade 12 General Weighted Average (GWA) of 


--- PAGE 2 ---
at least 93.00% or equivalent, with no subject grade lower than 86.00%, and grades of at 
least 90.00% in high school subjects aligned with the chosen college degree. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 93.00% (Entry GWA cutoff). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Requires documentary proof 
of financial status via Income Tax Return or Certificate of Indigency; live database records 
a PHP 350,000.00 ceiling). 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Strictly restricted to designated partner State Universities and 
Colleges. 
●​ Priority Courses: Degree programs aligned with bank operations, including Accountancy, 
Business Administration, Finance, Information Technology, Computer Science, Data 
Analytics, Communications, and Journalism. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must NOT be a child or dependent of a Security Bank employee, 
and must not have an immediate family member within the second degree of 
consanguinity or affinity holding an active SBFI grant. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character from high school). - Health 
Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Must carry the full academic load prescribed by the university 
curriculum per term. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating Senior High School Grade 12 students entering 1st year 
college and continuing 1st year SUC students. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: No (unless applying for designated continuing SUC 
slots). 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced annually (typically Q1/Q2 prior to the academic year opening). 
●​ Closing Date: Specified in annual call for applications. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027. 
### 6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Full or partial tuition and matriculation fee coverage paid directly to the partner 
state university. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into the annual educational grant package. 
●​ Allowance: Financial assistance package up to PHP 50,000.00 – PHP 60,000.00 per 
academic year. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into the educational grant package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 


--- PAGE 3 ---
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None mandatory; direct hiring opportunities provided upon graduation. 
●​ Other Benefits: Scholar development workshops, leadership training, internship 
placements, and corporate career placement assistance within Security Bank 
Corporation. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Duly accomplished SBFI Online Application Form. 
2.​ High School Transcript of Records / Grade 12 Report Card showing final GWA. 
3.​ Proof of Admission or Acceptance Letter from partner State University. 4. Proof of 
Financial Status (Latest Parent Income Tax Return, Certificate of Indigency, or OFW 
Contract). 
4.​ PSA Birth Certificate of student applicant. 6. Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
5.​ School Grading System documentation (if non-percentage system). 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 86.00% or 
equivalent by the end of each academic term. - Regular Load: Enrolled in the full unit load 
prescribed by the university curriculum. 
●​ No Failures: No grade lower than 80.00% (or equivalent) in any academic subject per 
term. 
●​ Return Service: None mandatory. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Being a child or dependent of an employee of Security Bank Corporation or its 
subsidiaries. 
●​ Having an immediate relative within the second degree of consanguinity or affinity holding 
an active SBFI scholarship. 
●​ Term GWA dropping below 86.00% or receiving a subject grade below 80.00%. 
●​ Enrolling in a non-partner state university or non-aligned degree program.
