13. Matching Risks 
●​ Academic Cutoff Discrepancy: The live production database records a minimum GWA of 
85.00%. Relying on 85.00% yields false-positive matches for candidates who fail the 
mandatory 93.00% entry threshold established in provider guidelines. 
●​ Institutional Restriction: Candidates admitted to non-partner public universities will be 
rejected during screening despite satisfying academic marks. 
14. Verification Summary 
●​ Verification Status: Verified. 
●​ Last Verified Date: 2026-08-05. 
●​ Official URLs Checked: https://www.securitybank.com/foundation, 
https://integratedreport.securitybank.com. 
●​ Broken Links: None. 
●​ Overall Confidence Score: 88/100. 
Caritas Manila YSLEP Scholarship Program (ID: 59) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Youth Servant Leadership and Education Program (YSLEP) 
●​ Provider: Caritas Manila, Inc. 
●​ Category: Private / Faith-Based / Need-and-Merit 
●​ Official Website: https://caritasmanila.org.ph 
●​ Application Portal: Caritas Manila YSLEP Secretariat / Partner Diocesan Offices / NDMU 
CMRE Office 
●​ Official Guidelines: Caritas Manila YSLEP Application Guidelines and Servant Leadership 
Formation Manual 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
YSLEP serves as the flagship human development initiative of Caritas Manila, operating on the 
principle that education coupled with values formation provides an effective pathway out of 
systemic poverty. The program finances tertiary and technical-vocational education while 
instilling Christian values, civic responsibility, and servant leadership to transform scholars into 
productive, ethically grounded community leaders. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: Resident of Metro Manila (Archdiocese of Manila) or any of the 53 partner 
dioceses nationwide. 


--- PAGE 6 ---
●​ Education Level: Tertiary (College Undergraduate) and Technical-Vocational (TVET). 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Years 1, 2, 3, 4, and 5. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in an accredited university, 
college, or TVET institution. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Senior High School overall GWA of at least 85.00% or 
equivalent. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% (Entry and retention cutoff). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Requires a Certificate of 
Indigency from the Barangay or DSWD verifying financial distress; live database specifies 
PHP 180,000.00). 
●​ Age Restrictions: Must be between 18 and 25 years old at the time of application. 
●​ School Restrictions: Accredited partner institutions within participating dioceses. 
●​ Priority Courses: Open to all degree programs; includes a dedicated track 
(YSLEP-GEN129) prioritizing Agriculture degrees and sustainable farming. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be single; must be an active member of a Basic Ecclesial 
Community (GKK) or Parish Youth Ministry. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character). 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Zero-tolerance policy regarding vices (smoking, alcohol 
consumption, substance abuse, gambling, and computer game addiction); must NOT hold 
a Grant-in-Aid Working Student (GIA/WS) award from the school. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Single Senior High School graduates and continuing college or TVET 
students aged 18 to 25. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes. 
### 5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Set annually by local diocesan YSLEP secretariats. 
●​ Closing Date: Determined per participating diocese. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Direct financial grant covering matriculation and tuition fees. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into the overall annual educational allowance. 
●​ Allowance: Annual financial support package valued at approximately PHP 30,000.00 – 
PHP 35,000.00 per scholar. 


--- PAGE 7 ---
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into the annual grant package. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into the annual grant package. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: Post-graduation pledge ("Balik-Handog"): Join the Caritas YSL Alumni 
Association (CAMASA) and donate 1% of gross monthly salary (or a minimum of PHP 
100.00/month) upon employment to finance future scholars. 
●​ Other Benefits: Monthly Servant Leadership formation workshops, spiritual retreats, and 
community volunteerism programs. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Accomplished Caritas Manila YSLEP Application Form. 
2.​ Photocopy of SHS Report Card / College Transcript showing GWA \ge 85.00%. 
3.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
4.​ PSA Birth Certificate or Baptismal Certificate. 
5.​ Certificate of Indigency from Barangay or DSWD. 
6.​ Parents' Marriage Certificate (photocopy). 
7.​ Two (2) 2x2 ID photos. 
8.​ Interview clearance from local Screening Committee. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a general average of at least 85.00% per academic term. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit load; shifting degree programs without approval is barred. 
●​ No Failures: Passing grades in all enrolled subjects. 
●​ Return Service: Must attend monthly YSL formation workshops and render 50 hours of 
volunteer work per school year in parish or community apostolates. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Marriage, pregnancy, or exceeding 25 years of age. 
●​ Engagement in vices (smoking, alcohol, gambling, illegal drugs, or gaming addiction). 
●​ Holding a school Grant-in-Aid Working Student (GIA/WS) grant or major external award. 
●​ Term GWA dropping below 85.00% or unapproved shifting of degree program. 
●​ Failure to attend mandatory monthly YSL formation workshops or complete 50 annual 
volunteer hours.

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Database GWA Understatement: Live production database lists min_gwa: 80. Official 
guidelines mandate a strict 85.00% cutoff, generating false positives for applicants in the 
80.00%–84.99% bracket. 
●​ Civil Status and Behavioral Constraints: Engine matching must validate user age <= 25, 
civil status == 'Single', and parish activity.

AFPSLAI Educational Grant Program (ID: 62) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: AFPSLAI Educational Grant Program (EGP) — Non-Business Track 
●​ Provider: Armed Forces and Police Savings and Loan Association, Inc. (AFPSLAI) 
●​ Category: Private / Affiliation / Need-and-Merit 
●​ Official Website: https://www.afpslai.com.ph 
●​ Application Portal: Physical submission at nearest AFPSLAI branch office (forms 
downloadable online) 
●​ Official Guidelines: AFPSLAI Educational Grant Program (EGP) Policy Guidelines & Form 
EGP 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The AFPSLAI Educational Grant Program (EGP) is a corporate social responsibility initiative 
designed to assist active, retired, and deceased military and police personnel by subsidizing the 
tertiary education of their legal dependents. The program focuses on non-business degree 
programs to promote diverse professional development among military families. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming Freshmen), Year 2 (Sophomores), and Year 3 
(Juniors). 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (2nd and 3rd year college students; 4th year students are 


--- PAGE 10 ---
strictly barred). 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Accepted or enrolled in a non-business baccalaureate 
degree program. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Proof of highest educational attainment demonstrating passing 
academic standing. 
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Passing academic standing 
required; live database records 85.00%). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Total annual gross family income of the sponsor must not exceed PHP 
1,000,000.00. 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Recognized Philippine colleges and universities. 
●​ Priority Courses: Restricted EXCLUSIVELY to non-business baccalaureate courses (e.g., 
Computer Science, IT, Engineering, Social Sciences, Agriculture, Science). 
Business-related courses are covered under the separate Scholarship Apprentice 
Program). 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Sponsor must be an active, retired, or deceased Regular Member 
of AFPSLAI in good standing. Applicant must be a legitimate, illegitimate, or legally 
adopted child. If sponsor is single/unmarried without children, a legitimate/adopted sibling 
may apply. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character). 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Strictly limited to ONE (1) grantee per sponsor/family; applicant must 
NOT hold any other external scholarship grant (except school merit tuition discounts). 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Dependents (children or qualified siblings) of regular AFPSLAI members 
who are incoming freshmen, 2nd-year, or 3rd-year college students. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No (4th-year students are ineligible). 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: No (if another family member was a prior grantee). 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced annually (typically May/June). 
●​ Closing Date: Specified per cycle (e.g., June 26 or July 15). 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. - Current AY Covered: AY 2026–2027. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Up to PHP 30,000.00 per school term (and up to PHP 10,000.00 for mandatory 
summer/midyear terms). 
●​ Monthly Stipend: PHP 4,000.00 per month. 


--- PAGE 11 ---
●​ Allowance: ROTC incentive of PHP 2,000.00 per month during terms when ROTC is 
enrolled. 
●​ Book Allowance: Up to PHP 2,000.00 per school term (reimbursement basis). 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None required for EGP track (unlike the SAP business track which 
requires a 6-to-11 month service bond). 
●​ Other Benefits: Board and lodging subsidy up to PHP 2,000.00/month for SUC students 
when necessary. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Duly accomplished AFPSLAI EGP Application Form. 
2.​ PSA Birth Certificate of applicant. 
3.​ Report Cards / Certified True Copy of Grades / TOR. 
4.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
5.​ Valid Police Clearance or NBI Clearance of applicant (Barangay clearance strictly barred). 
6. Parent/Sponsor Proof of Income: Latest payslip / Certificate of Pension (COP) AND 
latest Income Tax Return (ITR) for both parents. 
6.​ Official Local Government Certificate of No Income / Affidavit if parent is unemployed. 
7.​ PSA CENOMAR and Affidavit of No Child (if sponsor is a sibling). 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain passing academic standing per university curriculum. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment. 
●​ No Failures: No failing or dropped subjects. - Return Service: None. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Total gross annual family income exceeding PHP 1,000,000.00. 
●​ Being in the 4th year level or pursuing a graduate degree. 
●​ Enrolling in a business-related degree program under the EGP track. 
●​ Application where a family member / sibling was a previous AFPSLAI scholar. 
●​ Dual enjoyment of external non-school scholarship grants. 
●​ Submission of Barangay clearance instead of Police/NBI clearance.

Security Bank External Scholarship Program (ID: 71) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Security Bank Foundation Scholars for Better Communities Scholarship 
Program (External) 
●​ Provider: Security Bank Foundation, Inc. (SBFI) 
●​ Category: Private / Corporate Foundation / Merit-and-Need 
●​ Official Website: https://www.securitybank.com/foundation 
●​ Application Portal: https://www.securitybank.com/foundation 
●​ Official Guidelines: SBFI External Scholarship Program Guidelines 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The External Scholarship Program provides financial assistance to high-performing students 
enrolled in SBFI partner private and public higher education institutions. The program targets 
courses directly supporting banking, corporate management, technology, and communications, 
cultivating talent for the national economy and Security Bank's workforce pipeline. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Live database records NCR region 
filter). 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming Freshmen) and limited upperclassmen in 
designated partner HEIs. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: Primary entry is incoming 1st year; limited upperclassmen 
slots exist per university. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (limited slots). 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible. 


--- PAGE 14 ---
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Accepted or enrolled in any of SBFI's 8 partner 
universities (Ateneo de Manila University, De La Salle University, Far Eastern University, 
Polytechnic University of the Philippines, University of Santo Tomas, etc.). 
●​ Academic Requirements: High school Grade 12 GWA of at least 93.00% or equivalent; no 
subject grade lower than 86.00%; grades of 90.00%+ in subjects aligned with college 
course. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 93.00% (Entry) / 86.00% (Continuation). 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Requires parent ITR, 
Indigency Certificate, or OFW Contract). 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Strictly restricted to SBFI's 8 partner universities. - Priority Courses: 
Business Administration, Finance, Accountancy, Financial Management, Information 
Technology, Computer Science, Data Analytics, Communications, and Journalism. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must NOT be a child of a Security Bank employee, and must not 
have an active SBFI scholar relative within the second degree. 
●​ Good Moral: Required. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Must maintain full-time credit enrollment per term. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating SHS Grade 12 students and continuing 1st-year students at 
partner HEIs. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: No (unless occupying open continuing slots). 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. - Can previous applicants reapply?: NOT SPECIFIED IN 
OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced per annual cycle. 
●​ Closing Date: Announced per annual cycle. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Full or partial tuition and matriculation fee coverage paid directly to the university. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into educational grant package. 
●​ Allowance: Annual educational allowance package provided. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into grant package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 


--- PAGE 15 ---

●​ Return Service: None mandatory.

and career opportunities within Security Bank Corporation.

5.​ PSA Birth Certificate. 
6.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 7. School Grading System and Course Curriculum. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Term GWA of at least 86.00%. 
●​ Regular Load: Enrolled in prescribed full unit load per term. 
●​ No Failures: No grade lower than 80.00% in any subject. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions

●​ Having a relative within the second degree of consanguinity/affinity actively holding an 
SBFI scholarship.

The Regalo Mo, Kinabukasan Ko (RMKK) Scholarship Program provides educational 
assistance to third-party agency personnel (security guards, janitorial, maintenance, and 
messengerial staff) deployed across Security Bank offices and their dependents. The initiative 
supports scholars from elementary through college, promoting socio-economic mobility and 
offering direct bank employment upon graduation.

●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE.

Gokongwei Brothers Foundation STEM-College 
Scholarship (ID: 72) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: GBF STEM-College Scholarship (Formerly GBF-Gokongwei Group STEM

●​ Official Website: https://www.gokongweibrothersfoundation.org

●​ Application Portal: https://bit.ly/GBFSTEMCollege 
●​ Official Guidelines: GBF STEM-College Scholarship Program Guidelines 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The GBF STEM-College Scholarship supports academically outstanding Filipino students 
pursuing higher education in Science, Technology, Engineering, and Mathematics (STEM). By 
combining direct financial grants with leadership development and employment opportunities 
across Gokongwei Group business units, the program seeks to build STEM expertise and 
advance national industrial competitiveness. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: Resident of the Philippines. 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 1 (Incoming Freshmen) and Year 2+ (Continuing College 
Students). 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (2nd year and above). 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible for College Track (Separate TeachSTEM Master's exists). 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or planning to enroll in a priority STEM degree 
program in a recognized Philippine university. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Minimum overall General Weighted Average (GWA) of 85.00% 
or 2.0 (or equivalent); incoming freshmen must belong to the Top 10% of their Senior High 
School graduating batch. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% or 2.0. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: Top 10% of SHS batch for incoming freshmen. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Must demonstrate financial need; proof of annual household income 
required (Live database specifies PHP 400,000.00 cap). 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Philippine colleges and universities offering accredited priority STEM 
programs. 
●​ Priority Courses: GBF Priority STEM Courses including Chemical Engineering, Civil 
Engineering, Computer Engineering, Electrical Engineering, Electronics Engineering, 
Industrial Engineering, Mechanical Engineering, Materials Engineering, Mining 
Engineering, Geodetic Engineering, Computer Science, Information Technology, Data 
Science, Chemistry, Accountancy, Animal Science, and Avionics Technology. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: None. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character). 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Must have NO failing (5.0), dropped, or incomplete (INC) grades in 
high school or prior college semesters; must not hold another major corporate scholarship 
grant. 
4. Application Timing 


--- PAGE 22 ---
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating Grade 12 SHS students and continuing college students 
(2nd year and above) enrolled in priority STEM programs. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes (as incoming 1st year or entering 2nd year). 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced annually (typically Q1/Q2). 
●​ Closing Date: May 31, 2026 (for AY 2026–2027 cycle). 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–2027. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Covered as part of the direct financial grant package. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into the annual financial grant. 
●​ Allowance: Direct annual financial grant of PHP 80,000.00 to PHP 120,000.00 per year 
(depending on the university, directly credited to scholar's bank account). 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into grant package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: Mandatory return service obligation (render service in the Philippines or 
within Gokongwei Group companies equal to scholarship period). 
●​ Other Benefits: Leadership training, upskilling programs, OJT/internship placement within 
Gokongwei Group partner companies, and priority employment opportunities upon 
graduation. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Certified True Copy of Grade 12 Report Card (for Freshmen) OR Certified True Copy of 
Grades for last 2 consecutive semesters (for Upperclassmen). 
2.​ Certificate of Class Rank (Top 10% certification for incoming freshmen). 
3.​ Notice of Admission or Proof of University Application. 
4.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
5.​ Proof of Household Income (Parent ITR, Certificate of Employment with salary, OFW 
contract, or BIR Tax Exemption Certificate). 
6.​ Proof of Billing (Utility bill matching residence address). 
7.​ Recommender's email address (Non-relative reference). 
### 8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a term GWA of at least 85.00% or 2.0. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment in approved STEM program. 


--- PAGE 23 ---
●​ No Failures: Zero failing, incomplete, or dropped grades. 
●​ Return Service: Compliance with post-graduation return service agreement. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Enrolling in non-STEM degree programs. 
●​ Term GWA dropping below 85.00% or receiving a failing, dropped, or incomplete mark. 
●​ Failure to submit proof of top 10% class rank for incoming freshman entry. 
●​ Holding an overlapping major corporate scholarship. 
10. Temporal Eligibility Matrix

Aboitiz Brights / Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship 
(ID: 75) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship Program (AFLSP) / Aboitiz Brights 
●​ Provider: Aboitiz Foundation, Inc. 
●​ Category: Private / Corporate Foundation / Merit-based 


--- PAGE 25 ---
●​ Official Website: https://aboitiz.com/aboitiz-foundation/aboitiz-future-leaders-scholarship 
●​ Application Portal: 
https://sites.google.com/aboitiz.com/aboitiz-future-leaders-scholar/home AND University 
Scholarship Portals (e.g., UP SIKAP portal https://sikap.upd.edu.ph) 
●​ Official Guidelines: Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship Program Guidelines AY 
2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The Aboitiz Future Leaders Scholarship Program, established in 2000, serves as the flagship 
college scholarship program of the Aboitiz Foundation. Aligned with UN Sustainable 
Development Goal 4, the program provides full financial support, executive mentorship, 
leadership development, and internship opportunities to develop high-potential university 
sophomores into ethical leaders. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Must be a Filipino citizen. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Year 2 (Incoming Sophomores ONLY). 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: NO (Incoming Freshmen are strictly INELIGIBLE). 
●​ Existing College Students?: YES (Strictly restricted to incoming 2nd-year college 
students). 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled as an incoming sophomore student at a 
designated partner university. 
●​ Academic Requirements: First-year college GWA/GPA of at least 88.00% or 2.0 (or 
equivalent); NO dropped subjects, NO failing grades (5.0), and NO unremoved 
incomplete (INC) or 4.0 grades in any academic subject. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 88.00% or 2.00. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Requires submission of 
household financial proof or Affidavit of Income). 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to designated partner universities: Ateneo de 
Manila University, De La Salle University, Mapúa University, University of Santo Tomas, 
UP Diliman, UP Baguio, UP Cebu, UP Los Baños, and UP Mindanao. 
●​ Priority Courses: Engineering (Electrical, Industrial, Civil, Chemical, Computer, 
Mechanical, Materials, Mining, Geodetic, Electronics), Data Science, Computer Science, 
BA Communication / Journalism, BS Agriculture, BS Forestry, BS Psychology. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: None. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character; no record of any form of 
disciplinary action). 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Applicants receiving scholarships from other corporate foundations 
are ineligible (CHED and DOST scholars are allowed). 


--- PAGE 26 ---
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Incoming 2nd-year college (sophomore) students enrolled in 
pre-identified courses at partner universities. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: No (Must complete 1st year to apply as an incoming 
sophomore). 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes (Primary and exclusive target applicant cohort). 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (if entering sophomore year). 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: August 1 annually. 
●​ Closing Date: September 1 annually. 
●​ Application Cycle: Fixed / Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: Full 100% tuition and matriculation fee coverage. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: PHP 10,000.00 per month. 
●​ Allowance: Board Exam Review Fee allowance of PHP 15,000.00. 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into monthly stipend package. 
●​ Transportation: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: PHP 15,000.00 one-time review grant. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: Incentives for academic honors: 
○​ Academic Excellence Incentive: PHP 20,000.00 (One-time). 
○​ Latin Honor Incentive: Summa Cum Laude (PHP 10,000.00), Magna Cum Laude 
(PHP 7,000.00), Cum Laude (PHP 5,000.00). 
○​ Board Topnotcher Incentive: Provided. 
●​ Return Service: No mandatory employment return service bond; scholars must complete 
a mandatory 400-hour internship within Aboitiz Group business units and attend 
leadership development sessions. 
●​ Other Benefits: Mentorship from Aboitiz executives, learning development sessions, and 
networking opportunities. 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Copy of Student ID. 
2.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character. 
3.​ Certified True Copy of Grades / Transcript of Records covering full 1st year college. 
4.​ Certificate of Enrollment / Registration Form for 1st and 2nd term of current SY. 
5.​ Copy of Certificates of College Leadership, Awards, or Seminars. 


--- PAGE 27 ---
6.​ Proof of Household Income / Affidavit of Income Source. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain required term GWA (at least 88.00% / 2.0). 
●​ Regular Load: Full credit load per term. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing grades (5.0), dropped marks (DRP), or unremoved incomplete 
(INC/4.0) grades. 
●​ Return Service: Complete mandatory 400-hour Aboitiz Group internship and participate in 
leadership events. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Being an incoming freshman, 3rd-year, or 4th-year college student. 
●​ Enrolling in a non-partner university or non-identified degree program. 
●​ Having any grade of 5.0, DRP, or unremoved INC/4.0 mark in 1st year college. 
●​ Holding an active scholarship grant from another corporate foundation. 
●​ First-year GWA dropping below 88.00% or 2.0.

Canonical Scholarship Verification 
Report: UniFAST and CHED Priority 
Programs 
Systemic verification of higher education funding programs requires rigorous alignment with Tier 
1 primary evidence, including government decrees, joint memorandum circulars, and official 
provider guidelines. Operational scholarship matching engines depend on deterministic 
validation to eliminate false-positive recommendations that misdirect applicants and 
false-negative omissions that suppress viable funding opportunities. 
This canonical verification audit evaluates five higher education financial assistance programs 
administered by the Commission on Higher Education (CHED) and the Unified Student 
Financial Assistance System for Tertiary Education (UniFAST): the Tulong Dunong Program (ID 
5), the Tertiary Education Subsidy (ID 66), the SIDA Scholarship Grant Program (ID 118), the 
Estatistikolar Program (ID 119), and the SIKAP Faculty Grant (ID 120). 
Tulong Dunong Program (TDP) (ID: 5) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: CHED-UniFAST Tulong Dunong Program (CHED-TDP / UniFAST-TDP) 
●​ Provider: Unified Student Financial Assistance System for Tertiary Education (UniFAST) / 
Commission on Higher Education (CHED) 
●​ Category: Government / National / Undergraduate / Need-based Grant-in-Aid 
●​ Official Website: https://unifast.gov.ph 
●​ Application Portal: https://unifast.gov.ph/tes.html 
●​ Official Guidelines: CHED-DBM Joint Memorandum Circular (JMC) No. 04, s. 2019; 
UniFAST Memorandum Circular No. 02, s. 2026 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
The CHED-UniFAST Tulong Dunong Program provides targeted financial assistance to qualified 
and deserving Filipino tertiary students enrolled in public or private higher education institutions. 
Designed as a grant-in-aid initiative under the Tertiary Education Subsidy (TES) umbrella 
framework, the program supplements the direct and indirect costs of higher education, including 
living allowances, books, and essential educational expenses. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Year 


--- PAGE 2 ---
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be enrolled in a first undergraduate degree in 
State Universities and Colleges (SUCs), CHED-recognized Local Universities and 
Colleges (LUCs) with Certificate of Program Compliance (COPC), or Private Higher 
Education Institutions (HEIs) with COPC or listed in the CHED Registry 
●​ Academic Requirements: Senior High School report card / Form 138 (for incoming 
freshmen) or a certified true copy of grades for the latest semester attended (for ongoing 
college students) 
●​ Minimum GWA: 75.00% (passing grade) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined annual gross family income of parents or legal guardians 
must not exceed ₱400,000.00 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted to SUCs, CHED-recognized LUCs with COPC, or Private 
HEIs with COPC / included in the CHED Registry of Programs 
●​ Priority Courses: Any recognized undergraduate degree program 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Good Moral: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Applicants must not be availing of multiple national 
government-funded educational grants, except for Free Higher Education under Republic 
Act 10931, DSWD AICS, or Student Monetary Assistance for Recovery and Transition 
(SMART) 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating high school students, incoming college freshmen, and 
ongoing undergraduate college students 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (grantees must reapply or re-confirm qualification 
annually per academic cycle) 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Varies by CHED Regional Office (CHEDRO) and partner institution 
schedule 
●​ Closing Date: September 30 per JMC guidelines / as announced by regional advisories 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual (disbursed semestrally at ₱7,500.00 per term) 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2024–2025 / AY 2025–2026 
6. Benefits 


--- PAGE 3 ---
●​ Tuition: Included in the financial grant (up to ₱15,000.00 per AY; serves as a direct 
subsidy in SUCs/LUCs where tuition is covered under RA 10931) 
●​ Monthly Stipend: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Integrated into general living 
allowance) 
●​ Allowance: ₱15,000.00 per academic year (₱7,500.00 per semester) 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into general educational grant 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into living subsidy 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Return Service: None 
●​ Other Benefits: Direct educational grant-in-aid supplementing Free Higher Education 
7. Required Documents 
1. Fully accomplished UniFAST-TDP Application Form (Annex 2) 2. Certificate of Enrollment 
(COE) or Certificate of Registration (COR) 3. Certificate of Indigency issued by the Barangay, 
latest BIR Form 2316 / Income Tax Return, BIR Certificate of Tax Exemption, or Social Case 
Study Report 4. Academic Record: Form 138 / SF9 for incoming freshmen, or Certified True 
Copy of Grades for the latest semester attended for ongoing students 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a passing General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 75.00% 
●​ Regular Load: Carry a regular academic credit load per semester as determined by the 
HEI 
●​ No Failures: Maintain passing standing across all enrolled subjects 
●​ Return Service: None 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Concurrent enjoyment of major national government educational scholarship grants 
(excluding RA 10931 Free Tuition and DSWD AICS) 
●​ Combined annual family gross income exceeding ₱400,000.00 
●​ Enrollment in non-COPC degree programs or non-recognized private institutions 
●​ Failure to maintain a passing GWA or regular credit load

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Program Compliance Risk: Applicants enrolled in HEI programs lacking COPC 
accreditation will be rejected during CHED regional review. The matching engine must 
validate school_id and program_copc_status prior to displaying TDP recommendations. 
●​ Grant Duplication Risk: Students benefiting from major national grants (e.g., CHED Merit, 
DOST) are disqualified. The matching pipeline must check 
user.active_government_grants.

●​ Last Verified Date: 2026-08-05 
●​ Official URLs Checked: https://unifast.gov.ph, https://legacy.ched.gov.ph, 
https://www.dbm.gov.ph 
●​ Broken Links: None 
●​ Overall Confidence Score: 96/100 
CHED-UniFAST Tertiary Education Subsidy (TES) (ID: 
66) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: CHED-UniFAST Tertiary Education Subsidy (TES) (enacted under 
Republic Act No. 10931, Universal Access to Quality Tertiary Education Act) 
●​ Provider: Unified Student Financial Assistance System for Tertiary Education (UniFAST) / 
Commission on Higher Education (CHED) 
●​ Category: Government / National / Undergraduate / Need-based Subsidy 
●​ Official Website: https://unifast.gov.ph 
●​ Application Portal: UniFAST Portal / HEI-administered Portal 
●​ Official Guidelines: CHED-UniFAST-DBM Joint Memorandum Circular No. 01 s. 2017; 
JMC No. 04 s. 2020; Memorandum Circular No. 1 s. 2021 (TES-3B) 
●​ Current Status: Active 


--- PAGE 6 ---
2. Purpose 
The Tertiary Education Subsidy (TES) provides comprehensive financial support to qualified, 
socioeconomically disadvantaged Filipino students pursuing undergraduate degrees in SUCs, 
LUCs, and private HEIs. Established under RA 10931, the subsidy covers tuition and 
matriculation in private institutions (TES-1), living allowances and learning materials (TES-2), 
additional allowances for students with disabilities (TES-3A), and licensure examination 
reimbursements (TES-3B). 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: Priority given to students residing and studying in municipalities without public 
university campuses 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, and 5th Year 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be enrolled in an undergraduate degree program 
in an SUC, CHED-recognized LUC, or private HEI included in the UniFAST Registry 
●​ Academic Requirements: Passing GWA and regular academic credit load per term 
●​ Minimum GWA: 75.00% (passing grade per HEI retention standards) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Dynamically evaluated based on DSWD Listahan 2.0 / 4Ps household 
income ranking and poverty threshold deciles 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: SUCs, LUCs with institutional recognition, or private HEIs in the 
official UniFAST Registry 
●​ Priority Courses: Any recognized undergraduate degree program 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Priority given to 4Ps / Listahan households, Persons with 
Disabilities (TES-3A), and students in municipalities without public SUC/LUC campuses 
●​ Good Moral: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Evaluated via UniFAST prioritization hierarchy: (1) Continuing 
StuFAPs grantees, (2) Listahan 2.0 / 4Ps ranked households, (3) Municipality exclusivity 
applicants 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Enrolled undergraduate students submitted by their respective HEIs 
during official UniFAST portal intake calls 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No (except claiming TES-3B licensure reimbursement within 2 


--- PAGE 7 ---
years of graduation) 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes (continuing grantees are re-validated semestrally) 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Set by UniFAST per academic billing cycle 
●​ Closing Date: Specified in UniFAST regional advisories 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual prioritization / Semestral disbursement 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2024–2025 / AY 2025–2026 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: TES-1 covers full tuition and school fees in private HEIs (up to ₱20,000.00/sem or 
₱40,000.00/AY) 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Integrated into living allowance (TES-2) 
●​ Allowance: TES-2 living allowance: up to ₱40,000.00 per AY (₱20,000.00 per semester) 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into TES-2 allowance 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into TES-2 allowance 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: TES-3B provides up to ₱10,000.00 one-time reimbursement for 
licensure review and examination fees 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Return Service: None 
●​ Other Benefits: TES-3A provides an additional ₱30,000.00 per AY for Persons with 
Disabilities (PWDs) 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Certificate of Enrollment / Registration Form (COR) from UniFAST-registered HEI 
2.​ DSWD 4Ps / Listahan Household ID, or Barangay Certificate of Indigency 
3.​ Valid Student ID / Government ID 
4.​ For TES-3A: PWD ID issued by NCDA / LGU 
5.​ For TES-3B: PRC Official Receipt, Review Center Receipt, and Notarized Letter of Intent 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a passing GWA per semester according to HEI retention rules 
●​ Regular Load: Enrolled in a regular credit load per term 
●​ No Failures: Compliance with academic standing requirements 
●​ Return Service: None 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Enrollment in non-registered private HEIs or non-compliant programs 
●​ Exceeding the maximum residency period of the degree program 
●​ Failure to maintain passing GWA or academic dismissal from the HEI 
●​ Possession of an earned post-secondary or college degree 


--- PAGE 8 ---

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Unregistered Private HEI Risk: Recommending TES to students in unregistered private 
institutions will cause disqualification. The engine must check 
scho[span_297](start_span)[span_297](end_span)[span_299](start_span)[span_299](end
_span)ol.is_unifast_registered. 
●​ Income Evaluation Mismatch: Income is evaluated dynamically via DSWD Listahan 
deciles rather than a static numeric cap. Setting 
inc[span_301](start_span)[span_301](end_span)[span_303](start_span)[span_303](end_
span)ome_limit: null while evaluating user.is_listahan_registered prevents mis-filtering.

Scholarship Grant Program for Children and 
Dependents of Sugarcane Industry Workers and Small 
Sugarcane Farmers (SIDA-SGP) (ID: 118) 


--- PAGE 10 ---
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Scholarship Grant Program for Children and Dependents of Sugarcane 
Industry Workers and Small Sugarcane Farmers (SIDA-SGP) 
●​ Provider: Commission on Higher Education (CHED) in partnership with the Sugar 
Regulatory Administration (SRA) under Republic Act No. 10659 (Sugarcane Industry 
Development Act of 2015) 
●​ Category: Government / National / Need-based / Sectoral Agriculture Grant 
●​ Official Website: https://legacy.ched.gov.ph/sida-sgp/ 
●​ Application Portal: SRA Mill District Offices / CHED Regional Office Portals 
●​ Official Guidelines: CHED Memorandum Order (CMO) No. 30, s. 2016; CMO No. 02, s. 
2020 (Amendments); CMO No. 15, s. 2026 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
SIDA-SGP provides educational assistance to qualified children and dependents of sugarcane 
industry workers and small sugarcane farmers. Mandated by RA 10659, the program supports 
undergraduate and graduate studies in agriculture, agricultural engineering, chemical 
engineering, and sugar technology offered at State Universities and Colleges (SUCs) to build 
human capital for the Philippine sugarcane industry. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: Resident of sugarcane-producing mill districts in the Philippines 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate (College) and Graduate (Master's / Doctoral) 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Undergraduate: Years 1–4; Graduate: Years 1–3 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes (with earned units relevant to priority degree programs) 
●​ Graduate Students?: Yes (Master's and Doctoral degree levels) 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in an identified State University 
and College (SUC) 
●​ Academic Requirements: SHS report card / Form 138 (for freshmen), certified true copy 
of grades for latest semester (for college), or TOR & Diploma (for graduate applicants) 
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Selection ranked 70% 
Academic + 30% Income; passing GWA required for retention) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined annual gross income of parents/guardian must not exceed 
₱400,000.00 for Undergraduate track; combined annual gross income of 
applicant/spouse/parents must not exceed ₱500,000.00 for Graduate track 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to identified State Universities and Colleges 
(SUCs) 
●​ Priority Courses: Agriculture, Agricultural Engineering and Mechanics, Chemical 
Engineering, Sugar Technology, and related ladderized programs specified under Sec. 
6(b) of RA 10659 


--- PAGE 11 ---
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be certified by the Sugar Regulatory Administration (SRA) 
as legitimate children or dependents of sugarcane industry workers or small sugarcane 
farmers 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character) 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Applicants must submit complete documents to their local SRA Mill 
District office to secure SRA certification prior to CHEDRO ranking 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating SHS students, ongoing college students with earned units, 
and graduate applicants admitted to SUCs 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can graduates apply?: Yes (for Master's/Doctoral programs) 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Set annually per CHEDRO and SRA advisory 
●​ Closing Date: Announced per academic cycle 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: SUC tuition is free under RA 10931 for undergraduates; Graduate track provides 
up to ₱60,000.00 per AY (₱30,000.00 per semester) for TOSF 
●​ Monthly Stipend: ₱10,000.00 per month (₱100,000.00 per 10-month AY) for both 
Undergraduate and Graduate tracks 
●​ Allowance: Integrated into monthly stipend 
●​ Book Allowance: ₱10,000.00 per AY (₱5,000.00 per semester) 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into monthly stipend package 
●​ Insurance: Medical insurance integrated into stipend package 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Thesis proposal required 
for graduate track entry) 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Return Service: Mandatory 1 year of return service in the Philippines for every 1 year of 
scholarship availed, completed within 2 years after graduation (prioritizing government 
agencies directly working with the sugarcane industry, other government offices, or 
related private entities) 
●​ Other Benefits: Total financial package value: ₱110,000.00/AY for Undergraduate track; 
₱175,000.00/AY for Graduate track 


--- PAGE 12 ---
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Official SRA Certification confirming applicant as child/dependent of a sugarcane worker 
or small sugarcane farmer 
2.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character 
3.​ Notice of Admission / Certificate of Registration from participating SUC 
4.​ Academic Record: Form 138 / SF9 (for SHS), Certified True Copy of Grades for latest 
semester (for college), or TOR & Diploma (for graduate applicants) 
5.​ Proof of Income: Latest Income Tax Return (ITR) or BIR Certificate of Tax Exemption 
(Income 
\l[span_341](start_span)[span_341](end_span)[span_389](start_span)[span_389](end_sp
an)e ₱400,000 for undergrad; \le ₱500,000 for grad) 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain passing GWA per semester as prescribed by SUC retention 
policies 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit load in approved priority degree program 
●​ No Failures: Maintain regular academic standing without failing grades 
●​ Return Service: Signed commitment to render 1:1 return service post-graduation 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Absence of official Sugar Regulatory Administration (SRA) certification 
●​ Combined gross family income exceeding ₱400,000.00 (undergraduate) or ₱500,000.00 
(graduate) 
●​ Enrollment in non-priority programs or private (non-SUC) institutions 
●​ Academic failure or dismissal from the SUC

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Missing Sectoral Restriction Risk: The live database lacks an explicit tag for SRA 
certification. The matching engine must validate user.is_sra_certified_dependent == true 
to prevent non-sugarcane applicants from matching. 
●​ Institution Type Restriction: The program is strictly confined to SUCs. school_type must 
be restricted to SUC instances.

CHED Scholarship Program for Future Statisticians 
(Estatistikolar) (ID: 119) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: CHED Scholarship Program for Future Statisticians (Estatistikolar) 
●​ Provider: Commission on Higher Education (CHED) in cooperation with the Philippine 
Statistics Authority (PSA) 
●​ Category: Government / National / Undergraduate / Merit-and-Need / Priority Field 
●​ Official Website: https://legacy.ched.gov.ph/estatistikolar/ 
●​ Application Portal: CHED Regional Office Online estatistikolar portals (e.g., 
https://bit.ly/2026EstatistikolarApplicationPortal) 
●​ Official Guidelines: CHED Memorandum Order (CMO) No. 14, s. 2025; Memorandum 
OED No. 1064, s. 2026 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
Estatistikolar addresses the national shortage of statisticians and data scientists by providing 
financial assistance to qualified students pursuing undergraduate statistics degrees. Developed 
under CMO No. 14 s. 2025 in collaboration with the PSA, the program supports students in BS 
Statistics and BS Applied Statistics to strengthen human capital for national data infrastructure 
and policy planning. 


--- PAGE 15 ---
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: Undergraduate / College 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: 1st, 2nd, 3rd, and 4th Year - Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Yes 
●​ Graduate Students?: No 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Enrolled or accepted in BS Statistics, BS Applied 
Statistics, or PSA-identified statistics programs in private HEIs with Government 
Recognition (GR) or SUCs/LUCs with COPC/IR 
●​ Academic Requirements: SHS GWA of at least 85.00% or equivalent for incoming 
freshmen; minimum college GWA of 80.00% or equivalent for 2nd to 4th-year college 
students 
●​ Minimum GWA: 85.00% (Incoming Freshmen / Grade 12); 80.00% (Ongoing 2nd–4th 
Year College Students) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined annual gross income of parents or legal guardians must not 
exceed ₱500,000.00 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE ("covers college Filipino 
students regardless of age...") 
●​ School Restrictions: Private HEIs with Government Recognition (GR), State Universities 
and Colleges (SUCs), or Local Universities and Colleges (LUCs) with IR/COPC 
●​ Priority Courses: Bachelor of Science in Statistics, Bachelor of Science in Applied 
Statistics, or programs specifically identified by the Philippine Statistics Authority (PSA) 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Special equity groups (PWDs under RA 7279, Magna Carta for 
Poor under RA 11291, NCIP IPs, DHSUD Underprivileged/Homeless, First-Generation 
students) receive +5 bonus points in ranking 
●​ Good Moral: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Other Official Rules: Ranking criteria: 70% Academic Performance + 30% Annual Gross 
Income (+5 equity bonus points). Must execute a notarized scholarship contract (Annex 
B-2). 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: SHS graduating students, incoming 1st-year college students, and 
ongoing 2nd, 3rd, and 4th-year college students enrolled in BS Statistics/Applied 
Statistics 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes 


--- PAGE 16 ---
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: June 22, 2026 (for AY 2026–2027 intake) / June 15 for prior cycles 
●​ Closing Date: July 31, 2026 (for AY 2026–2027 intake) / August 15 for prior cycles 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–2027 (77 national slots authorized under CEB Res. 
374-2026) 
### 6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: SUCs/LUCs: Covered under Free Higher Education (RA 10931); Private HEIs: Up 
to ₱40,000.00 per AY (₱20,000.00 per semester) TOSF coverage 
●​ Monthly Stipend: ₱7,000.00 per month (₱35,000.00 per semester = ₱70,000.00 per 
10-month AY) 
●​ Allowance: Integrated into monthly stipend 
●​ Book Allowance: ₱10,000.00 per AY (₱5,000.00 per semester for book/connectivity 
allowance) 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into living stipend 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Return Service: Maintain minimum GWA of 80% during study; complete degree within 
prescribed period; no explicit mandatory post-grad public service years specified in CMO 
14 s. 2025 contract, but scholar must adhere to contract terms 
●​ Other Benefits: Total financial package value per AY: ₱80,000.00 in SUCs/LUCs (stipend 
+ book allowance); ₱120,000.00 in Private HEIs (tuition + stipend + book allowance) 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Fully accomplished online Estatistikolar Application Form (Annex A) 
2.​ Proof of Citizenship: Birth Certificate issued by NSO/PSA 
3.​ Academic Record: Form 138/SF9 (GWA \ge 85% for freshmen) or Certified True Copy of 
Grades for latest semester (GWA \ge 80% for 2nd–4th year) 
4.​ Proof of Income: Latest Income Tax Return (ITR) of parents/guardian, BIR Certificate of 
Tax Exemption/Non-Filer, OFW Contract/Proof of Income, or Social Case Study Report 
(Income \le ₱500,000) 
5.​ Special Equity Proof (if applicable): DHSUD/MSWDO Indigent Certificate, NCIP IP 
Certificate, PWD ID, or Social Case Study for First-Gen/Magna Carta for Poor 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a minimum General Weighted Average (GWA) of at least 80.00% 
or equivalent each semester - Regular Load: Carry regular academic load per term based 
on curriculum 
●​ No Failures: Maintain regular academic standing per CHEDRO monitoring 
●​ Return Service: Secure written approval prior to LOA, transfer, or shifting 


--- PAGE 17 ---
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Enrollment in non-statistics degree programs 
●​ Combined parent gross annual income exceeding ₱500,000.00 
●​ Freshmen SHS GWA below 85.00% or ongoing college GWA below 80.00% 
●​ Unauthorized shifting, school transfer, or unexcused Leave of Absence

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Missing Degree Constraint Risk: Recommending Estatistikolar to general science or 
mathematics students will result in instant rejection. Engine must enforce 
degree_program_restricted. 
●​ Dual GWA Logic Handling: Incoming freshmen require 85% while ongoing upperclassmen 
require 80%. The engine logic must evaluate minimum_gwa dynamically based on 
user.year_level.

Scholarships for Instructors' Knowledge 
Advancement Program (SIKAP) (ID: 120) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Scholarships for Staff and Instructors' Knowledge Advancement Program 
(SIKAP) 
●​ Provider: Commission on Higher Education (CHED) 
●​ Category: Government / National / Graduate / Faculty Development / Institutional 
Capacity Building 
●​ Official Website: https://sikap.ched.gov.ph 
●​ Application Portal: SIKAP Online Portal (https://sikap.ched.gov.ph) 
●​ Official Guidelines: CHED Memorandum Order (CMO) No. 06, s. 2020; CMO No. 28, s. 


--- PAGE 19 ---
2021 (Revised Guidelines for SIKAP Grant) 
●​ Current Status: Active 
2. Purpose 
SIKAP provides advanced graduate study opportunities to teaching and non-teaching personnel 
of Philippine higher education institutions (HEIs). Established under CMO No. 28 s. 2021, the 
program funds Master's and Doctoral degree studies in CHED-recognized Delivering Higher 
Education Institutions (DHEIs) to upgrade faculty qualifications, foster research output, and 
improve tertiary instruction quality nationwide. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Filipino citizen 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Education Level: Graduate (Master's and Doctorate degree levels) 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Incoming and ongoing Master's and Doctoral graduate students 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No 
●​ Existing College Students?: Ineligible (Restricted to post-baccalaureate graduate 
students) 
●​ Graduate Students?: Yes (Primary target cohort) 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be admitted or enrolled in a Master's or Doctoral 
program at a CHED-recognized Delivering Higher Education Institution (DHEI) 
●​ Academic Requirements: Bachelor's degree (for Master's track) or Master's degree (for 
Doctorate track); official endorsement from sending HEI 
●​ Minimum GWA: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Governed by DHEI graduate 
admission and retention standards) 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Income Ceilings: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Income uncapped; targeted 
at employed HEI personnel) 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE (Subject to sending HEI 
retirement policies) 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to CHED-recognized Delivering Higher Education 
Institutions (DHEIs) offering authorized graduate programs 
●​ Priority Courses: Graduate degree programs in priority disciplines identified by CHED 
(e.g., STEAM, Teacher Education, Social Sciences, Health Sciences) 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Must be an active full-time or part-time faculty member or 
non-teaching personnel of a recognized Philippine HEI (sending HEI) 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Endorsement by sending HEI President/Head) 
●​ Health Requirements: Physically and mentally fit for graduate study 
●​ Other Official Rules: Requires a tripartite scholarship contract between CHED, Scholar, 
Sending HEI, and DHEI. Full-time contractual or Contract of Service faculty are eligible 
under Package B. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Active HEI faculty and non-teaching personnel pursuing Master's or 
Doctoral degrees 


--- PAGE 20 ---
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: Ineligible (Undergraduate freshmen cannot apply) 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: Ineligible 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: Ineligible 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Ineligible 
●​ Can graduates apply?: Yes (Bachelor's or Master's degree holders entering/enrolled in 
graduate school) 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Set annually per CHED call for applications 
●​ Closing Date: Announced per submission cycle 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual / Semestral intake 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–2027 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: 100% coverage of Actual Tuition and Other School Fees (TOSF) paid directly to 
the DHEI 
●​ Monthly Stipend: Living allowance based on study track (Full-Time vs Part-Time Package 
A/B): Master's up to ₱25,000.00–₱30,000.00/month; Doctorate up to 
₱35,000.00–₱40,000.00/month 
●​ Allowance: Learning materials, connectivity, and book allowance provided per term 
●​ Book Allowance: Integrated into semester learning materials allowance 
●​ Transportation: Provided for off-campus study/relocation if applicable 
●​ Insurance: Group health and accident insurance 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE 
●​ Research Allowance: Thesis grant (up to ₱50,000.00–₱60,000.00 for Master's) / 
Dissertation grant (up to ₱100,000.00 for Doctorate) 
●​ Graduation Allowance: Incentives for timely completion; mentor incentives paid to DHEI 
graduate advisor 
●​ Return Service: Mandatory return service rendered to the sending HEI (1 to 2 years of 
service for every 1 year of scholarship availed) 
●​ Other Benefits: Preservation of employment position and rank at sending HEI 
7. Required Documents 
1.​ SIKAP Application Form and Plantilla / Employment Verification from Sending HEI 
2.​ Official Nomination and Endorsement Letter from Sending HEI President/Head 
3.​ Proof of Admission / Registration in a CHED-recognized DHEI 
4.​ Certified True Copy of Transcript of Records (TOR) for previous degrees 
5.​ Curriculum Vitae (CV) and Research Concept Paper / Dissertation Work Plan 
6.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character / Clearance from sending institution 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain required passing GWA per DHEI graduate retention rules 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time or approved part-time credit load per approved curriculum plan 


--- PAGE 21 ---
●​ No Failures: Zero failing or incomplete grades in graduate coursework 
●​ Return Service: Fulfill binding return service contract post-graduation 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Non-employment as HEI faculty or non-teaching personnel 
●​ Enrollment in non-DHEI or non-approved graduate programs 
●​ Academic failure, unexcused dropping, or expulsion from the DHEI 
●​ Failure to secure official endorsement from the sending HEI

13. Matching Risks 
●​ Target User Misclassification Risk: In the production database, ID 120 lists levels: 
["Graduate", "Faculty"]. If an engine presents this grant to general undergraduate or 
non-HEI graduate students, it creates false-positive matches. The engine must enforce 
user.is_hei_employee == true. 
●​ Institution Type Validation: Applicants must be enrolled in recognized Delivering HEIs 
(DHEIs) rather than standard HEIs.

●​ Broken Links: None 
●​ Overall Confidence Score: 98/100 
Systemic Matching Architecture & Production 
Database Directives 
Analysis of governing policy guidelines across CHED and UniFAST reveals underlying structural 
patterns that dictate production schema design. Higher education funding in the Philippines 
relies heavily on sectoral certifications and institutional accreditations. Standard filter pipelines 
based solely on GWA and income are inadequate for preventing false recommendations. 
Multi-Stage Engine Matching Pipeline Architecture 
To enforce canonical accuracy, the matching engine routes student profile vectors through a 
three-stage sequential filtering pipeline: 


--- PAGE 23 ---
Pipeline Stage 
Evaluated Parameters 
Operational Logic & System 
Actions 
Stage 1: Hard Level & 
Temporal Filtering 
education_level, 
eligible_year_levels, 
incoming_year_only, 
requires_current_enrollment 
Evaluates whether applicant 
year_level is contained within 
eligible_year_levels. Enforces 
earned_college_units == 0 
when incoming_year_only is 
true, and verifies enrollment 
status against program 
mandates. 
Stage 2: Quantitative & 
Socioeconomic Bounds 
minimum_gwa, renewal_gwa, 
income_limit, 
rank_cutoff_alternative 
Validates applicant GWA 
against minimum_gwa and 
family income against 
income_limit. Implements 
dynamic branching for 
dual-GWA programs (such as 
Estatistikolar ID 119), applying 
an 85.00% threshold for 
incoming freshmen and an 
80.00% threshold for 
upperclassmen. 
Stage 3: Institutional, 
Sectoral & Course Matching 
school_type, priority_courses, 
degree_program_restricted, 
sectoral_restriction 
Matches institutional eligibility 
(SUC, LUC, Private COPC, or 
DHEI). Verifies degree program 
codes against restricted lists 
(e.g., BS Statistics for ID 119) 
and validates sectoral 
credentials (such as SRA 
certification for ID 118 or HEI 
employment for ID 120). 
Systemic Directives and Production Database Updates 
Secondary and third-order policy analysis demonstrates three vital structural requirements for 
database engineering: 
1.​ Sectoral Grants Depend on External Agency Registries: Programs such as SIDA-SGP (ID 
118) require verification against external government agency rosters (Sugar Regulatory 
Administration mill district rosters). Production databases must store explicit sectoral tags 
(sectoral_restriction) to avoid qualifying general agriculture students based solely on 
course title. 
2.​ Dual-GWA Architectures Require Temporal Evaluation: Programs like Estatistikolar (ID 
119) enforce distinct GWA thresholds depending on academic standing: 85.00% for 
incoming Grade 12 graduates and 80.00% for ongoing 2nd to 4th-year college students. 
Engine logic must branch dynamically based on user.year_level rather than evaluating a 
single static GWA column. 
3.​ Institutional Capacity Tags Prevent Disqualification: SIKAP (ID 120) requires enrollment in 
designated Delivering Higher Education Institutions (DHEIs), whereas TDP (ID 5) requires 
COPC accreditation. Production matching algorithms must validate 


--- PAGE 24 ---
school.accreditation_tags against scholarship requirements prior to output generation. 
Executing these database structural updates aligns the production system with canonical 
mandates, eliminating matching errors and delivering reliable recommendations to Filipino 
applicants.

Ateneo High School Financial Aid (ID: 18) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Ateneo Senior High School Financial Aid Grant1 
●​ Provider: Ateneo de Manila University (Ateneo Senior High School / Office of Admission 
and Aid)1 
●​ Category: Institutional / Secondary Education / Need-and-Merit1 
●​ Official Website: https://www.ateneo.edu/ashs/admissions/scholarships-financial-aid1 
●​ Application Portal: Integrated into the Ateneo Senior High School Admission and Financial 
Aid Portal1 
●​ Official Guidelines: Ateneo Senior High School Financial Aid Guidelines and Primer1 
●​ Current Status: Active3 
2. Purpose 
The Ateneo Senior High School Financial Aid Grant provides financial assistance to 
academically gifted but economically challenged Junior High School graduates from public, 
parochial, and private schools, facilitating equal access to quality secondary education and 
social mobility1. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen4. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: High School (Grade 11 entry)1. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Grade 111. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: Yes (Senior High School Grade 11 entry)1. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Ineligible1. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible1. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be a graduating Grade 10 student eligible for 
admission to Ateneo Senior High School1. 
●​ Academic Requirements: High academic performance in Junior High School with strong 
conduct marks1. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 90.00% (or equivalent high scholastic standing in JHS)3. 


--- PAGE 2 ---
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Family gross annual income must not exceed PHP 400,000.003. 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Open to graduates of public, parochial, and private Junior High 
Schools1. 
●​ Priority Courses: Applicable to all Ateneo Senior High School academic strands (STEM, 
ABM, HUMSS, GA)1. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: Priority given to public and parochial high school completers1. 
●​ Good Moral: Required (Certificate of Good Moral Character from JHS Principal)2. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Application for financial aid is evaluated independently of the 
academic admission decision6. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Graduating Grade 10 Junior High School students1. 
●​ Can current freshmen apply?: No (restricted to incoming Grade 11 SHS applicants)1. 
●​ Can current sophomores apply?: No. 
●​ Can current juniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: No. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: No. 
5. Application Window 
●​ Opening Date: Announced alongside the ASHS admission cycle (typically September)2. 
●​ Closing Date: Concurrent with the ASHS admission deadline (typically 
November/December)2. 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2026–20272. 
6. Benefits 
●​ Tuition: 100%, 75%, 50%, or 25% waiver of tuition and matriculation fees4. 
●​ Monthly Stipend: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Allowance: Food and student learning allowance provided based on evaluated need4. 
●​ Book Allowance: Book allowance provided for full-grant recipients4. 
●​ Transportation: Transportation subsidy provided for eligible commuters4. 
●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: Not applicable. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None required3. 
●​ Other Benefits: Dormitory assistance for qualified provincial scholars4. 


--- PAGE 3 ---
7. Required Documents 
1.​ Parent's Personal Letter detailing household background and financial hardship2. 
2.​ Father's and Mother's Income Tax Return (ITR), Certificate of Employment, or BIR Tax 
Exemption2. 
3.​ Utility bills (electricity, water, telephone) for the last three months2. 
4.​ Grade 10 Report Card / Form 1382. 
5.​ Certificate of Good Moral Character2. 
8. Renewal Requirements 
●​ Maintain GWA: Must maintain required academic and conduct marks specified by ASHS 
OAA2. 
●​ Regular Load: Full-time credit enrollment in assigned SHS strand2. 
●​ No Failures: Zero failing marks or major disciplinary infractions7. 
●​ Return Service: None. 
9. Disqualifying Conditions 
●​ Combined parent annual gross income exceeding PHP 400,000.003. 
●​ Submission of fraudulent income documents or altered report cards2. 
●​ Incurring failing grades or severe behavioral reprimands7.

UP Presidential Scholarship (ID: 24) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: UP Presidential Scholarship Program12 
●​ Provider: University of the Philippines System3 


--- PAGE 10 ---
●​ Category: Institutional / System-wide / Merit-and-Need3 
●​ Official Website: https://up.edu.ph / https://osfa.upd.edu.ph3 
●​ Application Portal: Integrated into the UP Student Learning Assistance System (SLAS 
Online)12 
●​ Official Guidelines: UP System Policy on System Scholarships; UP Gazette Vol. XXXIV12 
●​ Current Status: Active3 
2. Purpose 
To recognize and reward undergraduate students in the University of the Philippines System 
who demonstrate exceptional academic performance and leadership potential while delivering 
targeted financial relief12. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen12. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: College (Undergraduate)3. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Years 1, 2, 3, 4, and 53. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No12. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Eligible12. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Ineligible for undergraduate Presidential Scholarship (separate 
Graduate Presidential Fund exists)13. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be officially enrolled in a degree program in any 
UP constituent university12. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Outstanding scholastic record with a General Weighted 
Average (GWA) of at least 1.75 (or 95% equivalent)3. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 1.75 on the UP grading scale (95.00% equivalent)3. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Family gross annual income must not exceed PHP 400,000.003. 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to UP Constituent Universities (UPD, UPLB, UPM, 
UPV, UPC, UPMin, UPOU, UP Tacloban)12. 
●​ Priority Courses: Open across all undergraduate degree programs12. 
●​ Sectoral Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Good Moral: Required12. 
●​ Health Requirements: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Other Official Rules: Number of awards is limited by annual vacant slot allocations across 
constituent units12. 
4. Application Timing 
●​ Who May Apply: Enrolled UP undergraduate students12. 


--- PAGE 11 ---

rank)12.

●​ Can current juniors apply?: Yes12. 
●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes12. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: No. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes.

●​ Book Allowance: PHP 10,000.00 per academic year12. 
●​ Transportation: Integrated into monthly living allowance12. 
●​ Insurance: Access to UP Health Service medical benefits16. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: Thesis/dissertation allowance provided for graduating scholars14. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: Included in thesis completion support14. 
●​ Return Service: None required3. 
●​ Other Benefits: UP PGH hospitalization support up to PHP 150,000.00 annually16.

●​ Maintain GWA: Maintain a cumulative GWA of 1.75 or better each academic term12.

Ateneo Law School Financial Aid Program (ID: 42) 
1. Scholarship Identity 
●​ Official Name: Ateneo Law School Financial Aid Program3 
●​ Provider: Ateneo Law School (Ateneo de Manila University)3 
●​ Category: Institutional / Graduate / Law / Merit-and-Need3

●​ Official Guidelines: Ateneo Law School Financial Aid Policy and Guidelines3 
●​ Current Status: Active3 
2. Purpose 
To provide financial assistance to deserving law students pursuing the Juris Doctor (JD) degree 
at Ateneo Law School who exhibit high academic potential and financial need3. 
3. Official Eligibility Requirements 
●​ Citizenship: Natural-born or naturalized Filipino citizen5. 
●​ Residency: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Education Level: Graduate (Juris Doctor Program)3. 
●​ Eligible Year Levels: Years 1, 2, 3, and 4 (Juris Doctor)3. 
●​ Incoming Freshman Only?: No3. 
●​ Existing College Students?: Ineligible (Restricted to law students)3. 
●​ Graduate Students?: Yes (Juris Doctor is a professional law degree)3. 
●​ Current Enrollment Requirement: Must be admitted or enrolled in the Juris Doctor 
program at Ateneo Law School3. 
●​ Academic Requirements: Bachelor's degree completion, passing the Ateneo Law 
Admission Test (ALAT), and maintaining satisfactory academic standing3. 
●​ Minimum GWA: 82.00% (or equivalent law school QPI requirement)3. 
●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Income Ceilings: Combined family gross annual income must not exceed PHP 
600,000.003. 
●​ Age Restrictions: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ School Restrictions: Restricted strictly to Ateneo Law School (Rockwell Campus)3. 
●​ Priority Courses: Juris Doctor (JD) degree program3.

●​ Can current seniors apply?: Yes (4th year JD students)3. 
●​ Can graduates apply?: Yes (Bachelor's degree graduates entering law school)3. 
●​ Can previous applicants reapply?: Yes3.

●​ Closing Date: Set per annual law school calendar (typically June/July)3. 
●​ Application Cycle: Annual. 
●​ Current AY Covered: AY 2025–2026 / AY 2026–20273.

●​ Insurance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Board Exam Subsidy: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Research Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Graduation Allowance: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE. 
●​ Return Service: None3. 
●​ Other Benefits: Total estimated grant value up to PHP 120,000.00 per academic year3.

●​ Alternative Class Rank: NOT SPECIFIED IN OFFICIAL SOURCE.
